### Leveraging Pre-trained Large Language Models with Refined Prompting for Online Task and Motion Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21596v1)
- **Authors**: Huihui Guo, Huilong Pi, Yunchuan Qin, Zhuo Tang, Kenli Li
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of artificial intelligence, there is an increasing demand for intelligent robots capable of assisting humans in daily tasks and performing complex operations. Such robots not only require task planning capabilities but must also execute tasks with stability and robustness. In this paper, we present a closed-loop task planning and acting system, LLM-PAS, which is assisted by a pre-trained Large Language Model (LLM). While LLM-PAS plans long-horizon tasks in a manner similar to traditional task and motion planners, it also emphasizes the execution phase of the task. By transferring part of the constraint-checking process from the planning phase to the execution phase, LLM-PAS enables exploration of the constraint space and delivers more accurate feedback on environmental anomalies during execution. The reasoning capabilities of the LLM allow it to handle anomalies that cannot be addressed by the robust executor. To further enhance the system's ability to assist the planner during replanning, we propose the First Look Prompting (FLP) method, which induces LLM to generate effective PDDL goals. Through comparative prompting experiments and systematic experiments, we demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of LLM-PAS in handling anomalous conditions during task execution.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Leveraging Pre-trained Large Language Models with Refined Prompting for Online Task and Motion Planning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-PAS, a closed-loop task planning and acting system for robotic tasks assisted by a pre-trained Large Language Model (LLM).  LLM-PAS integrates traditional Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) with LLM reasoning capabilities. It shifts some constraint checking from the planning phase to the execution phase, allowing for more exploration of the constraint space and more accurate feedback on anomalies.  A key contribution is the "First Look Prompting" (FLP) method, which refines prompts to the LLM by focusing on anomaly-related information and contextual details, leading to more effective PDDL goal generation for replanning.  The authors demonstrate the system's effectiveness through simulations and real-world experiments, showing improved performance in handling anomalies compared to other LLM-based approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Integration of LLMs with TAMP:** The paper's strength lies in its successful integration of LLMs' reasoning abilities with traditional TAMP, addressing a crucial challenge in robotics: dealing with unexpected situations during task execution. This moves beyond simply using LLMs as a standalone planner, which are generally insufficient for complex TAMP problems.
    *   **First Look Prompting (FLP):** FLP appears to be a well-designed prompting method that mitigates the limitations of LLMs by focusing on the relevant anomaly information and providing a more targeted context. The ablation studies showcase the benefits of FLP in generating effective PDDL goals.
    *   **Closed-Loop System:** The closed-loop architecture enables the system to adapt and recover from anomalies by replanning. The authors validate LLM-PAS through experimental results which show that the system can recover from different types of anomalies compared to baseline methods. The work shifts away from offline or purely heuristic replanning strategies, which often struggle in unstructured and dynamic environments.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper presents a decent evaluation including simulations and real-world experiments. The comparison with other LLM-based reactive planners (InnerMono, ProgPrompt) provides evidence of the advantages of LLM-PAS and FLP. The demonstration of addressing anomalies in a real robotic setup further enhances the credibility of the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Anomalies:** While the paper demonstrates effectiveness in handling certain types of anomalies (object loss, action blocking, state change), the scope is relatively limited.  The complexity of the real-world demonstration, while useful, is not particularly high.  The system's ability to generalize to more complex and less predictable anomalies is not fully established.
    *   **Dependency on PDDLStream and CSubBT:** The system relies on PDDLStream for initial planning and CSubBTs for execution.  The performance of LLM-PAS is therefore inherently dependent on the limitations of these underlying components.  The paper would be strengthened by a discussion of how LLM-PAS interacts with or mitigates potential issues stemming from these tools.
    *   **Black-Box Nature of LLMs:** The LLM's reasoning process remains largely a black box.  It would be useful to have a deeper analysis of the types of reasoning performed by the LLM and how the FLP influences this reasoning.  More transparency into the LLM's decision-making could further improve the reliability and trustworthiness of the system.
    *   **Lack of Complexity Analysis:** While planning time is measured, there is no analysis of computational overhead introduced by the LLM module. This is especially important, since, given the time cost of prompting LLMs, such a system must be particularly efficient to be useful in real-time control scenarios.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The paper presents a useful contribution by combining LLMs and traditional TAMP to deal with real-world anomalies. The FLP method is a good and practical solution to deal with the challenge of prompting LLMs effectively for these complex scenarios. It is a step toward bridging the gap between high-level reasoning and low-level robotic control.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper addresses an important problem and presents a promising approach with FLP, the scope of anomalies considered and the lack of deep analysis of overheads limit its overall impact and novelty compared to previous research that dealt with anomaly detection using traditional control methods.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### RDF-Based Structured Quality Assessment Representation of Multilingual LLM Evaluations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21605v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Gwozdz, Andreas Both
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) increasingly serve as knowledge interfaces, yet systematically assessing their reliability with conflicting information remains difficult. We propose an RDF-based framework to assess multilingual LLM quality, focusing on knowledge conflicts. Our approach captures model responses across four distinct context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting, and no-context information) in German and English. This structured representation enables the comprehensive analysis of knowledge leakage-where models favor training data over provided context-error detection, and multilingual consistency. We demonstrate the framework through a fire safety domain experiment, revealing critical patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance, and demonstrating that our vocabulary was sufficient to express every assessment facet encountered in the 28-question study.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an RDF-based framework for structured quality assessment of Large Language Models (LLMs), especially focusing on their handling of knowledge conflicts and multilingual capabilities.  The authors propose a comprehensive RDF vocabulary aligned with FAIR principles to represent LLM evaluation results. The framework allows for analyzing model responses under various context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting, and no context) in both German and English. A fire safety domain experiment is used to demonstrate the framework's utility, revealing patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance. The results show that models tend to prioritize context even when incorrect, and demonstrate measurable differences in performance between languages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Gap:** The paper tackles a significant challenge in LLM evaluation: the lack of structured, standardized representations for assessing model quality, particularly in multilingual contexts and under knowledge conflict scenarios. The problem is a timely one, as LLMs increasingly become relied upon as knowledge interfaces.
*   **RDF-based Approach:** The use of RDF is a strong choice. It facilitates interoperability, reasoning, and integration with existing knowledge graphs. This makes the evaluation results findable, accessible, interoperable, and reusable (FAIR principles), contributing to a more systematic and shareable approach to LLM evaluation.
*   **Comprehensive Vocabulary:** The design of a specific RDF vocabulary for LLM evaluation is a valuable contribution. It provides a standardized way to represent key aspects of the evaluation process, including questions, answers, validation results, and context information.
*   **Multilingual Focus:** The explicit consideration of multilingual capabilities is important. It acknowledges that LLM performance can vary across languages, and that language-specific behaviors need to be captured in the evaluation process.
*   **Contextual Sensitivity:** The study evaluates LLMs under different context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting, no context). This provides a more nuanced understanding of how models handle knowledge conflicts and prioritize information from different sources.
*   **Demonstration Experiment:** The fire safety domain experiment offers a concrete example of how the framework can be applied. The analysis of the experimental results reveals interesting patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance. The observation that models often replicate incorrect context is valuable.
*   **Addresses reproducibility**: The code and full experimental details are made available in an online appendix.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Model Scope:** The experiment focuses on only two models, GPT-40-mini and Gemini-2.0-Flash. While the results are informative, it would be beneficial to extend the evaluation to a wider range of models to increase generalizability.
*   **Domain Specificity:** The fire safety domain, while important, may not be representative of all knowledge domains. Further experiments in other domains would strengthen the argument for the framework's broad applicability.
*   **Statistical significance:** Given the relatively small sample size (28 questions), the statistical significance of some of the observed differences may be limited. The authors acknowledge limitations regarding the exact McNemar test.
*   **Novelty**: The paper could be more strongly positioned within the existing landscape of LLM evaluation approaches. It's not entirely clear if the use of RDF for LLM eval is novel, or where other eval frameworks fall short.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty lies primarily in the combination of using RDF for representing LLM evaluation results, particularly for knowledge conflicts in a multilingual setting. While individual elements might have been explored previously, the integrated approach is novel. The significance stems from the potential to standardize and systematize LLM evaluation, leading to more reliable and reproducible results. The multilingual focus is also a significant contribution, as it addresses a critical aspect of LLM deployment in a global context. The paper also contributes a clearly defined vocabulary and a usable evaluation framework, supported by a well-documented experiment.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM evaluation by offering a structured, RDF-based framework that facilitates systematic analysis of model performance, particularly with respect to knowledge conflicts and multilingual capabilities.  The framework's adherence to FAIR principles enhances the reproducibility and reusability of evaluation results. The fire safety domain experiment provides a practical demonstration of the framework's utility, yielding interesting insights into context prioritization and language-specific behaviors.

However, the paper also has some limitations. The narrow scope of models and the relatively small sample size, could reduce the generalizability of the results.

Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field of LLM evaluation and has the potential to influence future research and practice in this area. The score reflects a paper that is well-executed and introduces a novel approach, but with some limitations that prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Meeseeks: An Iterative Benchmark Evaluating LLMs Multi-Turn Instruction-Following Ability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21625v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaming Wang
- **Abstract**: The ability to follow instructions accurately is fundamental for Large Language Models (LLMs) to serve as reliable agents in real-world applications. While existing instruction-following benchmarks are either single-turn or introduce new requirements in each turn without allowing self-correction, Meeseeks simulates realistic human-LLM interactions through an iterative feedback process. This design enables models to self-correct based on specific requirement failures, better reflecting real-world user-end usage patterns. The benchmark implements a comprehensive evaluation system with 38 capability tags organized across three dimensions: Intent Recognition, Granular Content Validation, and Output Structure Validation. Through rigorous evaluation across LLMs, Meeseeks provides valuable insights into LLMs' instruction-following capabilities in practical applications.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Meeseeks: An Iterative Benchmark Evaluating LLMs Multi-Turn Instruction-Following Ability" paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Meeseeks, a new multi-turn, automatic benchmark for evaluating the instruction-following capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike existing benchmarks that are either single-turn or provide new instructions at each turn, Meeseeks simulates realistic human-LLM interactions by allowing the LLM to self-correct based on iterative feedback. The benchmark uses a comprehensive evaluation system with 38 capability tags organized across three dimensions: Intent Recognition, Granular Content Validation, and Output Structure Validation. The authors also optimize the rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation process to reduce costs and improve accuracy. Experimental results demonstrate that Meeseeks provides valuable insights into LLMs' instruction-following capabilities in practical applications and their self-correction abilities under multi-turn scenarios. The evaluation is performed on a range of LLMs, showcasing strengths and weaknesses across various models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary strength of this paper lies in its multi-turn interactive evaluation framework. Existing instruction-following benchmarks primarily focused on single-turn scenarios or added new constraints with each turn, neglecting the important aspect of self-correction based on user feedback. Meeseeks directly addresses this gap by simulating realistic human-LLM interaction, and providing a valuable contribution to instruction-following benchmark research.

*   **Significance:** The ability of LLMs to self-correct and adapt to user feedback is crucial for real-world applications. Meeseeks enables a more nuanced assessment of LLMs, helping to identify specific areas where models struggle and understand how they improve with iterative feedback. The comprehensive set of capability tags provides a detailed breakdown of instruction-following performance, enabling researchers to pinpoint specific weaknesses.

*   **Technical Soundness:** The paper details the implementation of Meeseeks, including the extraction and evaluation processes. The optimization of the rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation approach to reduce costs and improve accuracy is also a relevant contribution. The use of both rule-based and LLM-based evaluation provides a balanced approach.

*   **Dataset and Experiments:** The paper's dataset size is sufficient, but more information on the creation and diversity of the data would improve confidence in the results. The data parameterization used is a valuable contribution. The evaluation includes a good range of LLMs, and the analysis of results, including performance differences between reasoning and non-reasoning models and their performance change with iterations, is insightful. However, the analysis is limited in depth.

*   **Limitations:** While the paper presents a strong contribution, some limitations exist. The automated evaluation system is susceptible to biases and errors inherent in LLM-based evaluators. While the paper tries to combat this with rules and optimizations, LLM-based evaluation can be prone to inaccuracies. Secondly, The evaluation is limited to three turns, which might not be sufficient to capture the full potential of iterative self-correction. While it highlights potential data leakage from using A-data directly, more detail would be helpful in future works. It would be beneficial to see Meeseeks used with different datasets and across more turns.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM evaluation by shifting the focus towards more interactive and realistic scenarios. The proposed evaluation framework could be adopted and extended by other researchers. Furthermore, the insights gained from Meeseeks could help guide the development of more robust and user-friendly LLMs. The careful handling of open-source dataset risk and encouragement for researcher engagement within AGI-Eval reflects a conscientious and beneficial approach.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation by introducing a multi-turn interactive benchmark. The comprehensive evaluation system and the optimization of the evaluation process further enhance the value of this work. While some limitations exist, the paper provides a valuable tool for assessing and improving the instruction-following capabilities of LLMs.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The score of 8 is justified by the following:

*   **High novelty:** The multi-turn interactive evaluation framework is a distinct improvement over existing benchmarks.
*   **Significant impact:** Meeseeks has the potential to influence future research in LLM evaluation and development.
*   **Technical soundness:** The paper details a well-designed and implemented evaluation system.
*   **Clear limitations:** The automated evaluation system may be prone to biases and errors in LLM evaluators, and there is a limited evaluation window.
*   **Room for improvement:** The dataset and evaluation of results could benefit from deeper analysis and more specific recommendations.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Sadeed: Advancing Arabic Diacritization Through Small Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21635v1)
- **Authors**: Zeina Aldallal, Sara Chrouf, Khalil Hennara, Mohamed Motaism Hamed, Muhammad Hreden, Safwan AlModhayan
- **Abstract**: Arabic text diacritization remains a persistent challenge in natural language processing due to the language's morphological richness. In this paper, we introduce Sadeed, a novel approach based on a fine-tuned decoder-only language model adapted from Kuwain 1.5B Hennara et al. [2025], a compact model originally trained on diverse Arabic corpora. Sadeed is fine-tuned on carefully curated, high-quality diacritized datasets, constructed through a rigorous data-cleaning and normalization pipeline. Despite utilizing modest computational resources, Sadeed achieves competitive results compared to proprietary large language models and outperforms traditional models trained on similar domains. Additionally, we highlight key limitations in current benchmarking practices for Arabic diacritization. To address these issues, we introduce SadeedDiac-25, a new benchmark designed to enable fairer and more comprehensive evaluation across diverse text genres and complexity levels. Together, Sadeed and SadeedDiac-25 provide a robust foundation for advancing Arabic NLP applications, including machine translation, text-to-speech, and language learning tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sadeed, a novel approach to Arabic diacritization based on fine-tuning a small, decoder-only language model (derived from Kuwain 1.5B). The model is trained on high-quality, curated diacritized datasets created through a robust data cleaning and normalization pipeline. The paper demonstrates that Sadeed achieves competitive results compared to larger, proprietary language models and outperforms traditional models trained on similar domains, despite using fewer computational resources. The authors also identify limitations in existing benchmarks for Arabic diacritization and propose SadeedDiac-25, a new benchmark designed to provide a fairer and more comprehensive evaluation across diverse text genres and complexity levels. They publicly release both the Sadeed model and the SadeedDiac-25 benchmark to promote further research. The paper includes detailed analysis of existing benchmarks (Abbad, CATT), a new diacritization dataset, and a thorough evaluation of Sadeed's performance compared to other models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a persistent and important problem in Arabic NLP: diacritization. Its strengths lie in the following areas:

*   **Model Efficiency:** The use of a small, fine-tuned language model (derived from Kuwain 1.5B), is a significant advantage. Demonstrating competitive performance with limited computational resources is valuable, promoting accessibility and reducing the environmental impact of large model training.

*   **Data Curation:**  The emphasis on creating high-quality training data and a novel benchmark is critical. The meticulous cleaning and normalization pipeline for the Tashkeela corpus, combined with expert review for SadeedDiac-25, addresses known issues with existing datasets and benchmarks. This is a major contribution, as dataset quality directly impacts model performance and evaluation reliability.

*   **Benchmark Analysis & Novel Benchmark:**  The detailed analysis of the Abbad and CATT benchmarks, highlighting overlap issues and annotation errors, is a key strength.  It exposes serious methodological problems in the field. The SadeedDiac-25 benchmark, aiming for balanced representation of Classical and Modern Standard Arabic, as well as diverse topics, represents a significant effort to improve evaluation practices.

*   **Public Release:**  The decision to publicly release both Sadeed and SadeedDiac-25 is commendable, fostering reproducibility and enabling further research by others.

*  **Language Injection**: The original language injection technique is unique and may open possibilities for other languages to follow.

However, there are also some weaknesses and areas for further work:

*   **Hallucination Rate:**  The paper acknowledges Sadeed's higher hallucination rate compared to other models. While this can be partially attributed to its smaller size, it needs further investigation.

*   **Limited MSA Training Data:** The reduced results on MSA content points to the need for MSA training data

*   **Benchmarking with open LLMs:** While the authors included benchmarking with large proprietary LLMs, they did not benchmark with other recent open LLMs such as Mistral or Llama.

*   **Limited novelty in architecture:** While the idea of fine-tuning a smaller model is indeed novel, there's a lack of innovation in the architecture; the small language model is not introduced nor experimented on in this paper.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The most significant contribution is the effort to address the severe data and evaluation shortcomings of the Arabic diacritization field. The rigorous cleaning and benchmark creation are more important than the specific model architecture. The release of both resources significantly advances the field. The approach of focusing on smaller, fine-tuned models can enable broader access to NLP technologies for Arabic. However, the method itself (fine-tuning a language model) is not particularly groundbreaking. The novelty exists in meticulously tackling the data quality issue and publicly sharing this resource.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper provides a solid contribution to Arabic diacritization, particularly through its emphasis on data quality and benchmark improvement. The small model's performance and the detailed benchmark analysis are valuable. The public release increases its impact. However, the novelty of the core method (fine-tuning) is limited, and some shortcomings in the model and benchmarking (hallucinations, benchmarking with SOTA open models) temper the overall score. The paper does not present any radically new architectural ideas. The score reflects a significant, valuable, and impactful but not exceptionally revolutionary contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion-based Adversarial Identity Manipulation for Facial Privacy Protection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21646v1)
- **Authors**: Liqin Wang, Qianyue Hu, Wei Lu, Xiangyang Luo
- **Abstract**: The success of face recognition (FR) systems has led to serious privacy concerns due to potential unauthorized surveillance and user tracking on social networks. Existing methods for enhancing privacy fail to generate natural face images that can protect facial privacy. In this paper, we propose diffusion-based adversarial identity manipulation (DiffAIM) to generate natural and highly transferable adversarial faces against malicious FR systems. To be specific, we manipulate facial identity within the low-dimensional latent space of a diffusion model. This involves iteratively injecting gradient-based adversarial identity guidance during the reverse diffusion process, progressively steering the generation toward the desired adversarial faces. The guidance is optimized for identity convergence towards a target while promoting semantic divergence from the source, facilitating effective impersonation while maintaining visual naturalness. We further incorporate structure-preserving regularization to preserve facial structure consistency during manipulation. Extensive experiments on both face verification and identification tasks demonstrate that compared with the state-of-the-art, DiffAIM achieves stronger black-box attack transferability while maintaining superior visual quality. We also demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach for commercial FR APIs, including Face++ and Aliyun.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "DiffAIM," a novel approach for facial privacy protection that leverages pre-trained diffusion models to generate natural adversarial faces. The core idea involves manipulating facial identity within the latent space of a diffusion model by iteratively injecting gradient-based adversarial guidance during the reverse diffusion process.  This guidance is carefully designed to balance identity convergence towards a target and semantic divergence from the source image to maintain naturalness. The method also incorporates a structure-preserving regularization to maintain facial structure consistency. The authors demonstrate that DiffAIM achieves strong black-box attack transferability and superior visual quality compared to state-of-the-art methods.  The effectiveness of DiffAIM is also validated on commercial FR APIs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its use of diffusion models for adversarial identity manipulation and its carefully crafted adversarial guidance objective. While previous works have explored diffusion models for adversarial examples, DiffAIM's specific approach to facial privacy, the integration of FR-driven and U-Net-driven objectives, and the structure-preserving regularization are contributions that differentiate it from existing methods. The adaptive ensemble strategy for enhancing black-box transferability is another noteworthy innovation. The identity-sensitive timestep truncation, while intuitively sound, is presented as a refinement to an already novel framework.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a relevant and growing concern regarding facial privacy in the age of widespread FR systems. The high degree of transferability achieved by DiffAIM is particularly significant because it improves the practicality of adversarial attacks against unknown, proprietary FR systems.  The superior visual quality compared to many existing methods is also important for real-world deployment where users are unwilling to compromise on image aesthetics.  Furthermore, this is achieved with the novel architecture provided in the paper.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results demonstrating DiffAIM's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods across multiple datasets, FR models, and commercial APIs. The ablation studies provide a solid understanding of the contribution of each component.
    *   **Well-Designed Approach:** The method is thoughtfully designed, combining FR objectives, U-Net semantic guidance, and structure regularization into a cohesive framework.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the approach, experiments, and results.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper conducts a thorough evaluation, considering both attack success rate and image quality, addressing concerns about visual artifacts often present in adversarial examples.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models are inherently computationally intensive, which can be a barrier to real-time or large-scale deployment.  Although the paper mentions timestep truncation to address this somewhat, the cost remains a consideration.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides a clear description of the method, it lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of why the proposed adversarial guidance and structure regularization are so effective.  Mathematical justifications could strengthen the paper.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper mentions the importance of the truncated timestep, ts. A more detailed analysis of the sensitivity of DiffAIM to other hyperparameters would improve its robustness.

*   **Potential Influence:** DiffAIM has the potential to significantly influence the field of facial privacy protection. The proposed techniques could be extended to other sensitive domains or used as a defense mechanism against adversarial attacks. The diffusion-based approach could inspire new lines of research in generating more natural and transferable adversarial examples.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

DiffAIM offers a significant advance in facial privacy protection by generating highly transferable and visually appealing adversarial faces using diffusion models. While the computational cost and lack of theoretical depth limit its impact somewhat, the strong empirical results, well-designed approach, and potential influence on the field justify a score of 8. This score reflects the paper's substantial contribution to the state-of-the-art and its potential to inspire further research in this important area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21650v1)
- **Authors**: Haiyang Zhou, Wangbo Yu, Jiawen Guan, Xinhua Cheng, Yonghong Tian, Li Yuan
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of diffusion models holds the promise of revolutionizing the application of VR and AR technologies, which typically require scene-level 4D assets for user experience. Nonetheless, existing diffusion models predominantly concentrate on modeling static 3D scenes or object-level dynamics, constraining their capacity to provide truly immersive experiences. To address this issue, we propose HoloTime, a framework that integrates video diffusion models to generate panoramic videos from a single prompt or reference image, along with a 360-degree 4D scene reconstruction method that seamlessly transforms the generated panoramic video into 4D assets, enabling a fully immersive 4D experience for users. Specifically, to tame video diffusion models for generating high-fidelity panoramic videos, we introduce the 360World dataset, the first comprehensive collection of panoramic videos suitable for downstream 4D scene reconstruction tasks. With this curated dataset, we propose Panoramic Animator, a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model that can convert panoramic images into high-quality panoramic videos. Following this, we present Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction, which leverages a space-time depth estimation method to transform the generated panoramic videos into 4D point clouds, enabling the optimization of a holistic 4D Gaussian Splatting representation to reconstruct spatially and temporally consistent 4D scenes. To validate the efficacy of our method, we conducted a comparative analysis with existing approaches, revealing its superiority in both panoramic video generation and 4D scene reconstruction. This demonstrates our method's capability to create more engaging and realistic immersive environments, thereby enhancing user experiences in VR and AR applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation" introduces a framework for generating immersive 4D scenes from either user-provided or model-generated panoramic images. It addresses the limitation of current diffusion models, which are primarily focused on static 3D scenes or object-level dynamics. The framework consists of three key components: (1) Panoramic Animator, a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model for converting panoramic images into high-quality panoramic videos; (2) Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction, a method to transform the generated panoramic videos into 4D point clouds using space-time depth estimation; and (3) a curated 360World dataset, which is a collection of fixed-camera panoramic videos used for training. The paper also details methods for improving panoramic video generation like Hybrid Data Fine-tuning (HDF) and Two-stage Motion Guided Generation (MGG), and Panoramic Circular Techniques (PCT). Experiments demonstrate the framework's ability to generate more engaging and realistic immersive environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The combination of panoramic video generation with 4D scene reconstruction is somewhat novel. While individual components build upon existing techniques, their integration for this specific application is a step forward. The 360World dataset is a valuable contribution given the scarcity of such data.  The proposed Panoramic Animator and the Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction techniques show some ingenuity.

* **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is significant in the VR/AR space. By providing a method for generating dynamic 4D scenes from panoramic images, the paper offers a path towards creating more immersive and interactive experiences. The work could potentially revolutionize how VR/AR content is created, making it more accessible and less reliant on complex and expensive manual scene authoring.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Dataset:**  The curated 360World dataset fills a gap in available resources for 4D scene generation from panoramic content.
    * **Comprehensive Framework:** The HoloTime framework offers a complete pipeline from image to 4D scene, addressing several critical challenges along the way.
    * **Experimental Validation:** The authors conduct both qualitative and quantitative experiments, including user studies, to validate their approach. Ablation studies further highlight the effectiveness of each component.
    * **Technical Contributions:** The proposed Panoramic Animator, specifically the Two-Stage Motion Guided Generation, and the space-time depth estimation method, appear to offer performance improvements over existing methods.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Improvements:** While the framework is novel in its integration, the individual components build upon existing techniques in video diffusion and 3D reconstruction. The degree of innovation in each individual component may be limited.
    * **Computational Cost:** 4D generation in general is computationally intensive, and there's no discussion of the computational challenges. The methods likely require significant resources for both training and inference. This could hinder wider adoption.
    * **Limited Scope of Motion:** While the paper aims to generate dynamic scenes, the types of motions generated (e.g., subtle movements, water flow) might still be somewhat constrained.
    * **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The method relies heavily on pre-trained models for both video diffusion and depth estimation. The performance of the framework is therefore limited by the quality and capabilities of these underlying models.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research in VR/AR content creation, particularly in generating dynamic 4D environments. The 360World dataset could become a valuable resource for training and evaluating new methods in this area. The techniques for panoramic video generation and space-time depth estimation could also inspire further research.

* **Justification of Score:** While HoloTime tackles an important problem with a complete pipeline and demonstrates good results, it's primarily an integration of existing techniques with some specific improvements. The novelty lies more in the system-level design and the dataset, rather than revolutionary advances in core technologies.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AdaR1: From Long-CoT to Hybrid-CoT via Bi-Level Adaptive Reasoning Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21659v1)
- **Authors**: Haotian Luo, Haiying He, Yibo Wang, Jinluan Yang, Rui Liu, Naiqiang Tan, Xiaochun Cao, Dacheng Tao, Li Shen
- **Abstract**: Recently, long-thought reasoning models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, but often incur substantial inference overhead, making efficiency a critical concern. Our empirical analysis reveals that the benefit of using Long-CoT varies across problems: while some problems require elaborate reasoning, others show no improvement, or even degraded accuracy. This motivates adaptive reasoning strategies that tailor reasoning depth to the input. However, prior work primarily reduces redundancy within long reasoning paths, limiting exploration of more efficient strategies beyond the Long-CoT paradigm. To address this, we propose a novel two-stage framework for adaptive and efficient reasoning. First, we construct a hybrid reasoning model by merging long and short CoT models to enable diverse reasoning styles. Second, we apply bi-level preference training to guide the model to select suitable reasoning styles (group-level), and prefer concise and correct reasoning within each style group (instance-level). Experiments demonstrate that our method significantly reduces inference costs compared to other baseline approaches, while maintaining performance. Notably, on five mathematical datasets, the average length of reasoning is reduced by more than 50%, highlighting the potential of adaptive strategies to optimize reasoning efficiency in large language models. Our code is coming soon at https://github.com/StarDewXXX/AdaR1
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AdaR1: From Long-CoT to Hybrid-CoT via Bi-Level Adaptive Reasoning Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the efficiency challenges of long-chain-of-thought (Long-CoT) reasoning in large language models (LLMs). The authors observe that Long-CoT doesn't always improve accuracy and can sometimes degrade it, especially for simpler problems.  They propose AdaR1, a two-stage framework for adaptive reasoning.  First, they create a "hybrid" reasoning model by merging Long-CoT and Short-CoT models. Second, they use a bi-level preference training approach. The group-level preference trains the model to choose between Long-CoT and Short-CoT based on the problem's complexity. Instance-level preference then encourages concise and correct reasoning *within* the chosen style. Experiments on math datasets demonstrate that AdaR1 significantly reduces inference costs (reasoning length) while maintaining or even improving performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strengths:** The core idea of adaptively choosing between different reasoning *styles* (Long-CoT vs. Short-CoT) is a significant step beyond previous work that primarily focuses on optimizing *within* a fixed Long-CoT paradigm. Bi-level preference training specifically designed for style selection and instance compression is a novel training methodology. The empirical analysis demonstrating the problem-dependent benefits of Long-CoT is also valuable in motivating the approach. The effort of merging and then finetuning for diverse CoT paths is non-trivial and adds to the novelty.
*   **Weaknesses:** Model merging itself is not a new technique, although its application in creating a hybrid reasoning model is interesting. The linear parameter merging strategy is quite simple; more sophisticated merging techniques could have been explored. Also, the idea of adaptive reasoning isn't entirely new (e.g., CoT-Valve), although this paper provides a more comprehensive approach and more compelling empirical results.

**Significance:**

*   **Strengths:**  The paper directly addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM deployment: inference efficiency.  If the demonstrated reductions in reasoning length translate to real-world cost savings, the impact could be substantial. The bi-level preference learning approach offers a potentially generalizable framework for other types of adaptive decision-making in LLMs (e.g., tool selection, knowledge source retrieval). The significant empirical improvements on standard math datasets (MATH, GSM8K) lend credibility to the approach. The results provide a new perspective about trade-off between accuracy and efficiency, showing a feasible way that reduces length with slight influence on accuracy.
*   **Weaknesses:** The experiments are primarily focused on mathematical reasoning tasks.  It's unclear how well AdaR1 would generalize to other domains (e.g., common-sense reasoning, question answering). While the paper mentions the goal is to move away from a long and redundancy CoT, the paper does not explain whether it indeed removes redundant information or what kind of information does it remove. Also, the selection of some parameters is heuristic and not well justified, such as the setting of 'epsilon' in group level preference.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM efficiency. The adaptive reasoning framework based on hybrid CoT models and bi-level preference training is a promising approach for reducing inference costs without sacrificing accuracy. The novelty lies in the combination of these elements and the demonstration of their effectiveness. However, the reliance on a relatively simple merging strategy, limited generalization evidence (mainly math datasets), and the existence of related, albeit less comprehensive, adaptive reasoning approaches (e.g., CoT-Valve) prevents it from reaching a higher score. Also, the justification on why the method can improve efficiency, as well as whether the reduction is caused by the removal of redundancy is not well-explained.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Precision to Perception: User-Centred Evaluation of Keyword Extraction Algorithms for Internet-Scale Contextual Advertising
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21667v1)
- **Authors**: Jingwen Cai, Sara Leckner, Johanna Björklund
- **Abstract**: Keyword extraction is a foundational task in natural language processing, underpinning countless real-world applications. A salient example is contextual advertising, where keywords help predict the topical congruence between ads and their surrounding media contexts to enhance advertising effectiveness. Recent advances in artificial intelligence, particularly large language models, have improved keyword extraction capabilities but also introduced concerns about computational cost. Moreover, although the end-user experience is of vital importance, human evaluation of keyword extraction performances remains under-explored. This study provides a comparative evaluation of three prevalent keyword extraction algorithms that vary in complexity: TF-IDF, KeyBERT, and Llama 2. To evaluate their effectiveness, a mixed-methods approach is employed, combining quantitative benchmarking with qualitative assessments from 552 participants through three survey-based experiments. Findings indicate a slight user preference for KeyBERT, which offers a favourable balance between performance and computational efficiency compared to the other two algorithms. Despite a strong overall preference for gold-standard keywords, differences between the algorithmic outputs are not statistically significant, highlighting a long-overlooked gap between traditional precision-focused metrics and user-perceived algorithm efficiency. The study highlights the importance of user-centred evaluation methodologies and proposes analytical tools to support their implementation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a user-centered evaluation framework for comparing different keyword extraction algorithms, focusing on their applicability in contextual advertising. It compares TF-IDF (a statistical method), KeyBERT (a deep learning method), and Llama 2 (a large language model) through a combination of quantitative precision metrics and qualitative user perceptions gathered via survey-based experiments. The study aims to identify key properties of high-quality keywords from a human perspective and to inform decisions about selecting keyword extraction algorithms for real-world applications, considering both effectiveness and computational efficiency.  The findings suggest a slight user preference for KeyBERT, striking a balance between performance and cost.  Importantly, the research highlights a potential disconnect between traditional precision-focused metrics and user-perceived algorithm efficiency, emphasizing the need for human-in-the-loop evaluation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its *methodological approach*. While individual keyword extraction algorithms and their performance have been extensively studied, the direct comparison using a robust user-centered evaluation framework, particularly in the context of contextual advertising and large language models, provides a fresh perspective. The development and application of 'horizontal' and 'vertical' analysis to assess question phrasing consistency during user studies are also notable contributions. However, the findings regarding the performance of the three chosen algorithms, KeyBERT, TF-IDF, and LLama2, while interesting, are in line with previous research, which diminishes the relative novelty of the performance comparisons.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical implications for the contextual advertising domain. By demonstrating the importance of user perceptions and the potential limitations of solely relying on traditional metrics, the research encourages a more nuanced and human-aware approach to algorithm selection.  The emphasis on computational cost and efficiency is also crucial in the context of high-volume, real-time advertising auctions. The insights can guide practitioners in making informed decisions about algorithm selection that balances user satisfaction, ad performance, and cost. Furthermore, the developed user-centered evaluation methodology itself is a valuable contribution that can be adopted in other related NLP tasks and domains. The research also points out the difficulty of measuring distinctiveness of keywords in an evaluation and suggests using accuracy instead, suggesting the usefulness of accuracy in future research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The study employs a mixed-methods approach, combining quantitative benchmarking with qualitative user surveys to understand the effectiveness of keyword extraction algorithms.
    *   **User-centered focus:** The work acknowledges the importance of human evaluation in the assessment of keyword extraction algorithms and provides practical guidance for incorporating user perceptions into the evaluation process.
    *   **Practical relevance:** The findings offer actionable insights for practitioners in the field of contextual advertising.
    *   **Well-defined research questions:** The research questions are clearly articulated and addressed throughout the paper.
    * The use of horizontal and vertical analysis is a strong addition to understanding the best phrasing of user-based evaluation questions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope:** The study focuses on a limited set of keyword extraction algorithms (TF-IDF, KeyBERT, Llama 2) and one specific application domain (contextual advertising). Generalizability may be limited.
    *   **Specific dataset:** Using only online news articles from The Guardian limits the scope of research due to the nature of text itself.
    *   **Challenges in Measuring "Distinctiveness":** The paper identifies and discusses a significant challenge in the consistent interpretation and measurement of "distinctiveness" as a quality metric for keywords. The results reveal lower ratings for distinctiveness and comments highlight a miss of nuanced tone and individual names.
    *   The comparison to a 'gold standard' dataset might have limited the scope of research, especially because some participants were likely to have similar keywords to the 'gold standard'.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in the areas of user-centered NLP evaluation and algorithm selection for practical applications. It also provides valuable insights for practitioners in the field of contextual advertising, informing their decision-making process and leading to improved user experiences. The identification of "distinctiveness" measurement limitations provides a guide for more research.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a rigorous, user-centered evaluation of keyword extraction algorithms, demonstrating the potential disconnect between traditional precision metrics and user perceptions. The methodology is sound, and the findings have practical implications for the contextual advertising domain. However, the limited scope of the algorithm comparison (only three methods and one specific type of text) and the somewhat constrained setting limits the generalizability of the conclusions. While the methodology is novel, the findings, in some aspects, are not groundbreaking, which limits its overall impact. Finally, the difficulties encountered with the 'distinctiveness' measurement suggest a need for further refinement. This suggests that, while significant, the paper doesn't fully meet the criteria for a higher score. While the evaluation framework is strong and valuable, the conclusions of the paper might be limited, decreasing the score from 8.0 to 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Traceback of Poisoning Attacks to Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21668v1)
- **Authors**: Baolei Zhang, Haoran Xin, Minghong Fang, Zhuqing Liu, Biao Yi, Tong Li, Zheli Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) integrated with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems improve accuracy by leveraging external knowledge sources. However, recent research has revealed RAG's susceptibility to poisoning attacks, where the attacker injects poisoned texts into the knowledge database, leading to attacker-desired responses. Existing defenses, which predominantly focus on inference-time mitigation, have proven insufficient against sophisticated attacks. In this paper, we introduce RAGForensics, the first traceback system for RAG, designed to identify poisoned texts within the knowledge database that are responsible for the attacks. RAGForensics operates iteratively, first retrieving a subset of texts from the database and then utilizing a specially crafted prompt to guide an LLM in detecting potential poisoning texts. Empirical evaluations across multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of RAGForensics against state-of-the-art poisoning attacks. This work pioneers the traceback of poisoned texts in RAG systems, providing a practical and promising defense mechanism to enhance their security.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces RAGForensics, a novel traceback system designed to identify poisoned texts within the knowledge database of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems.  RAG systems, while improving accuracy by leveraging external knowledge, are susceptible to poisoning attacks where malicious texts are injected to manipulate responses. RAGForensics addresses this vulnerability by iteratively retrieving subsets of texts from the database and employing a specifically crafted prompt to guide an LLM in detecting poisoned content. Empirical evaluations across multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of RAGForensics against various poisoning attacks, even including adaptive attacks specifically designed to circumvent the system.  The paper also proposes a method for identifying non-poisoned feedback and enhancing the accuracy of RAG outputs in these scenarios. It is the first work exploring traceback in RAG systems.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:* The primary strength of this paper is its novelty. It directly tackles a significant and relatively unexplored problem: tracing poisoned data in RAG systems. Current defenses mainly focus on mitigating the effects of poisoning during inference, which, as the paper points out, are insufficient against sophisticated attacks. Shifting the focus to traceback, identifying and removing the root cause, is a fundamentally different and valuable approach.  The application of LLMs in forensics is also a plus.

*Significance:*  The potential impact on the field is considerable.  By providing a mechanism for systematically identifying and removing poisoned data, the paper offers a practical and promising defense against poisoning attacks in RAG systems. This directly addresses a key security concern hindering the wider adoption of RAG in sensitive applications (e.g., legal, medical). The adaptive attacks created by the authors show that there is a need for robustness when creating traceback frameworks.

*Strengths:*
*   **First Mover Advantage:** The paper pioneers research in this area.
*   **Practical Focus:** The design incorporates realistic constraints like limited access to the underlying LLM and retriever parameters.
*   **Empirical Validation:** Thorough experiments across multiple datasets and attacks provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of RAGForensics. The adaptive attacks are important to show the robustness of the method.
*   **Addressing Non-Poisoned Feedback:** Addressing the case where incorrect responses are not due to poisoning is a thoughtful addition and makes the system more robust.

*Weaknesses:*
*   **Targeted Attacks:** The paper explicitly acknowledges that RAGForensics is currently limited to targeted poisoning attacks, where the attacker aims to influence the LLM for a specific query. Untargeted poisoning attacks are a limitation.
*   **Reliance on LLMs for Poison Detection:** The reliance on an LLM to classify texts as poisoned could introduce its own biases or vulnerabilities. While the authors attempt to mitigate this with structured prompts, the possibility of adversarial examples designed to fool the detection LLM remains. The choice of prompt could greatly affect the classification results.
*   **Computational Costs:** While the authors emphasize efficiency, the iterative nature of RAGForensics, involving multiple LLM queries, could still incur significant computational costs, especially for large knowledge databases. The algorithm could potentially benefit from heuristics to prioritize which texts to analyze first.

*Overall:* The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing a novel and practical approach to defending RAG systems against poisoning attacks. While it has some limitations, such as the focus on targeted attacks and reliance on LLMs for detection, the potential impact on the field is considerable. Further research building upon this work can address these weaknesses and extend the applicability of traceback methods to a wider range of poisoning scenarios.

Score: 8

*Justification:* A score of 8 reflects the paper's clear novelty and strong significance. The implementation and thorough evaluation strengthen its claim. Despite limitations regarding scope (targeted attacks) and reliance on LLMs, its innovative approach warrants a high rating. Future research, perhaps addressing untargeted attacks or exploring alternative detection methods, could raise this score higher.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Hoist with His Own Petard: Inducing Guardrails to Facilitate Denial-of-Service Attacks on Retrieval-Augmented Generation of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21680v1)
- **Authors**: Pan Suo, Yu-Ming Shang, San-Chuan Guo, Xi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with external knowledge bases, improving output quality while introducing new security risks. Existing studies on RAG vulnerabilities typically focus on exploiting the retrieval mechanism to inject erroneous knowledge or malicious texts, inducing incorrect outputs. However, these approaches overlook critical weaknesses within LLMs, leaving important attack vectors unexplored and limiting the scope and efficiency of attacks. In this paper, we uncover a novel vulnerability: the safety guardrails of LLMs, while designed for protection, can also be exploited as an attack vector by adversaries. Building on this vulnerability, we propose MutedRAG, a novel denial-of-service attack that reversely leverages the guardrails of LLMs to undermine the availability of RAG systems. By injecting minimalistic jailbreak texts, such as "\textit{How to build a bomb}", into the knowledge base, MutedRAG intentionally triggers the LLM's safety guardrails, causing the system to reject legitimate queries. Besides, due to the high sensitivity of guardrails, a single jailbreak sample can affect multiple queries, effectively amplifying the efficiency of attacks while reducing their costs. Experimental results on three datasets demonstrate that MutedRAG achieves an attack success rate exceeding 60% in many scenarios, requiring only less than one malicious text to each target query on average. In addition, we evaluate potential defense strategies against MutedRAG, finding that some of current mechanisms are insufficient to mitigate this threat, underscoring the urgent need for more robust solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Hoist with His Own Petard: Inducing Guardrails to Facilitate Denial-of-Service Attacks on Retrieval-Augmented Generation of LLMs" introduces a novel attack, MutedRAG, targeting Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. Unlike prior work that focuses on injecting malicious content to influence the output directly, MutedRAG exploits the LLM's own safety guardrails to trigger denial-of-service (DoS) attacks. This is achieved by injecting simple jailbreak prompts into the knowledge base, which, when retrieved in response to a user query, cause the LLM to refuse to answer, thus disrupting the system's availability.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of this approach across several datasets and LLMs, showing it requires fewer malicious texts and is more efficient than traditional poisoning methods. They also examine the resilience of MutedRAG against several defense strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel perspective on attacking RAG systems.  Existing research primarily concentrates on manipulating the content retrieved or directly influencing the LLM's output. MutedRAG's approach of repurposing the LLM's own safety mechanisms against it is a clever and previously unexplored attack vector. This qualifies as a significant conceptual contribution.

* **Significance:** The potential impact of this vulnerability is considerable. DoS attacks are critical because they directly affect system availability. MutedRAG's efficiency, requiring only a few malicious texts to impact many queries, amplifies this threat. High success rates reported across different LLMs and datasets further highlight the vulnerability's practical importance.  The finding that existing defense mechanisms are often insufficient underscores the need for a more robust RAG security.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and positions it effectively within existing research.
    * **Well-Defined Method:** The MutedRAG attack is well-described, including the suffix generation for refusal and prefix optimization modules for retrieval.  The two-stage optimization process is intuitive and justified.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** Experiments are extensive, using multiple datasets, LLMs, and evaluation metrics. Ablation studies are also included to understand the contribution of various components.
    * **Analysis of Defenses:** The evaluation of existing defense mechanisms is a critical strength. By identifying their limitations against MutedRAG, the paper clarifies the need for more targeted countermeasures.
    * **Reproducibility:** The detailed descriptions of the experimental setup and settings enhances the reproducibility of the research.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Defense Strategies:** The defense evaluation focuses primarily on adapting existing PoisonedRAG defenses, which, as the paper shows, are largely ineffective.  There's a lack of investigation into defenses specifically tailored to address the nuances of MutedRAG (e.g., techniques that detect or neutralize jailbreak prompts in retrieved contexts without compromising helpful content).
    * **Simplicity of Jailbreak Prompts:** While the simplicity contributes to the attack's effectiveness, further analysis into more sophisticated jailbreak prompts and their interaction with LLM guardrails could be investigated. Are there specific kinds of jailbreak prompt that work better, and why?
    * **Metrics Focus:** I-ASR metric only considers the queries affected by injected malicious texts. Consider also reporting the total number of queries blocked in the system. This would further demonstrate the attack's ability to disrupt the overall RAG.

* **Potential Influence:**  This paper is likely to spur further research in RAG security, specifically focusing on LLM guardrails and DoS vulnerabilities. It highlights a weakness that needs to be addressed to build more resilient RAG systems and may inspire new defense mechanisms against this type of attack.

* **Justification for Score:** The paper is novel in its attack vector, which is well-described and executed in the experiment. The comprehensive experimental design provides strong justification for the results. However, this paper can be improved by adding more tailor-made defense mechanism against the proposed attack vector.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Visual Text Processing: A Comprehensive Review and Unified Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21682v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Shu, Weichao Zeng, Fangmin Zhao, Zeyu Chen, Zhenhang Li, Xiaomeng Yang, Yu Zhou, Paolo Rota, Xiang Bai, Lianwen Jin, Xu-Cheng Yin, Nicu Sebe
- **Abstract**: Visual text is a crucial component in both document and scene images, conveying rich semantic information and attracting significant attention in the computer vision community. Beyond traditional tasks such as text detection and recognition, visual text processing has witnessed rapid advancements driven by the emergence of foundation models, including text image reconstruction and text image manipulation. Despite significant progress, challenges remain due to the unique properties that differentiate text from general objects. Effectively capturing and leveraging these distinct textual characteristics is essential for developing robust visual text processing models. In this survey, we present a comprehensive, multi-perspective analysis of recent advancements in visual text processing, focusing on two key questions: (1) What textual features are most suitable for different visual text processing tasks? (2) How can these distinctive text features be effectively incorporated into processing frameworks? Furthermore, we introduce VTPBench, a new benchmark that encompasses a broad range of visual text processing datasets. Leveraging the advanced visual quality assessment capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), we propose VTPScore, a novel evaluation metric designed to ensure fair and reliable evaluation. Our empirical study with more than 20 specific models reveals substantial room for improvement in the current techniques. Our aim is to establish this work as a fundamental resource that fosters future exploration and innovation in the dynamic field of visual text processing. The relevant repository is available at https://github.com/shuyansy/Visual-Text-Processing-survey.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of visual text processing, covering tasks like text image reconstruction (super-resolution, dewarping, enhancement) and manipulation (removal, editing, generation). It categorizes existing methods based on the textual features they utilize (structure, stroke, semantics, style) and the learning paradigms employed (architecture, loss functions, learning strategies, data representation).  The paper also introduces VTPBench, a new benchmark consisting of 4,305 samples across six sub-tasks, and VTPScore, an evaluation metric leveraging the visual quality assessment capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The paper conducts an empirical study of over 20 existing models on VTPBench and identifies open challenges and potential future research directions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary contribution lies in providing a comprehensive and structured review of visual text processing, a domain that has seen rapid growth but lacks a unified overview. The VTPBench benchmark and VTPScore metric also contribute new tools to the field. While existing surveys cover text detection and recognition, this work distinguishes itself by focusing specifically on *processing* tasks, offering a unique perspective. The use of MLLMs for evaluation is novel, but the specific implementation of VTPScore might need refinement.
*   **Significance:** Visual text processing is crucial for a range of applications, making advancements in this area highly impactful. A comprehensive review like this can accelerate research by providing a valuable resource for newcomers and experts alike. The identification of key features and learning paradigms allows researchers to strategically focus their efforts. The introduction of VTPBench aims to address the fragmentation of datasets and evaluation methodologies, fostering more rigorous and comparable research. The analysis of existing methods reveals gaps and opportunities for future work. The use of an MLLM for evaluation addresses some challenges, but it introduces new considerations.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey appears to cover a wide range of recent research in visual text processing.
    *   **Clear Categorization:**  The categorization of methods based on textual features and learning paradigms provides a useful framework for understanding the field.
    *   **Practical Contribution:** VTPBench and VTPScore provide concrete tools for evaluating and comparing different approaches.
    *   **MLLM Integration:** Using MLLMs for evaluation is a potentially powerful idea, leveraging their advanced capabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **MLLM Evaluation Dependence:** The reliance on MLLMs for evaluation introduces some bias and potential inconsistency. While GPT-4o is powerful, its judgment is not a perfect proxy for human perception and introduces a layer of opaqueness into the evaluation.
    *   **VTPScore Implementation Details:** The paper mentions that the MLLM responses are parsed for numerical values. A more detailed explanation of the prompting strategy and parsing mechanisms would add rigor to the evaluation.
    *   **Limited Real-World Impact Demonstrated:** While the paper presents a benchmark and metric, it lacks a thorough analysis of how applying these contributes to solving any particular application issue.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The survey has the potential to become a highly cited resource, guiding future research directions in visual text processing.  VTPBench, if adopted by the community, could lead to more standardized and comparable evaluations. The MLLM based metric might inspire development of stronger evaluation techniques

**Justification:**

The novelty of this paper is primarily the comprehensive overview of a less-explored area within the larger field of text detection and recognition. It provides a valuable resource to researchers in visual text processing, but some weaknesses with the benchmarks exist. This is balanced with the contribution to improved evaluation which is significant but not exceptional. It makes solid contributions but requires further development before having a major impact.
