- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### XBreaking: Explainable Artificial Intelligence for Jailbreaking LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21700v1)
- **Authors**: Marco Arazzi, Vignesh Kumar Kembu, Antonino Nocera, Vinod P
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models are fundamental actors in the modern IT landscape dominated by AI solutions. However, security threats associated with them might prevent their reliable adoption in critical application scenarios such as government organizations and medical institutions. For this reason, commercial LLMs typically undergo a sophisticated censoring mechanism to eliminate any harmful output they could possibly produce. In response to this, LLM Jailbreaking is a significant threat to such protections, and many previous approaches have already demonstrated its effectiveness across diverse domains. Existing jailbreak proposals mostly adopt a generate-and-test strategy to craft malicious input. To improve the comprehension of censoring mechanisms and design a targeted jailbreak attack, we propose an Explainable-AI solution that comparatively analyzes the behavior of censored and uncensored models to derive unique exploitable alignment patterns. Then, we propose XBreaking, a novel jailbreak attack that exploits these unique patterns to break the security constraints of LLMs by targeted noise injection. Our thorough experimental campaign returns important insights about the censoring mechanisms and demonstrates the effectiveness and performance of our attack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "XBreaking: Explainable Artificial Intelligence for Jailbreaking LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel jailbreaking attack, called XBreaking, which leverages Explainable AI (XAI) techniques to understand and exploit the behavioral differences between censored and uncensored Large Language Models (LLMs). XBreaking identifies key layers responsible for content filtering by comparing censored and uncensored model activations using XAI. It then injects targeted noise into these layers to bypass safety mechanisms and elicit harmful responses. The authors evaluate their method on four open-source LLMs, demonstrating its effectiveness in disrupting safety constraints while maintaining overall functionality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its use of XAI to *specifically* target layers responsible for content censorship in LLMs for jailbreaking. Existing methods primarily use black-box approaches to craft adversarial prompts or fine-tune entire models. The white-box XAI approach allows for a more precise and efficient attack strategy. By analyzing internal activations and attention mechanisms, the authors derive unique fingerprints for censored models, which is a novel contribution. The surgical noise injection guided by XAI also distinguishes it from generic noise-based adversarial attacks.
*   **Significance:** The paper contributes significantly to understanding the inner workings of LLM safety mechanisms. By identifying key censorship layers, it sheds light on how these models are aligned and restricted. This knowledge is valuable for both improving defenses and understanding vulnerabilities. The XBreaking attack demonstrates a practical and potentially scalable method for bypassing these safety measures, highlighting the importance of robust alignment techniques. It could influence future research in adversarial attacks and defense strategies for LLMs.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-defined methodology for XBreaking.
    *   The use of XAI is a clever way to gain insight into the black-box nature of LLMs.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough and covers multiple models.
    *   The authors analyze the impact of noise injection on both safety and functionality, a crucial consideration for practical attacks.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The threat model assumes white-box access, which may not always be realistic in real-world scenarios. This limits the applicability of the attack to situations where the model architecture and weights are available.
    *   The JudgeLLM evaluation, while providing a quantitative measure, relies on the quality of the LLM's judgment itself. While the author did try to validate the JudgeLLM, further validation would be helpful.
    *   The study's focus on a limited number of LLMs and harmful prompts may not generalize to all models and attack surfaces.
    * The results of the Qwen2.5 model were not thoroughly discussed, which is critical because it showed distinct differences in results.

*   **Impact:** While the paper explores a novel approach, its real-world impact could be limited by the white-box assumption. However, the insights gained into LLM safety mechanisms and the potential for XAI-guided attacks are valuable. It might inspire further research into more targeted adversarial methods and improved defense strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the white-box limitation, the paper presents a genuinely novel approach to jailbreaking LLMs by combining XAI and targeted noise injection. This provides valuable insights into how LLMs censor content and establishes a new avenue for adversarial research. While the immediate real-world applicability may be restricted, the methodology and findings are significant enough to warrant a score above average. The study's thorough evaluation also strengthens its contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Vision Transformers in Precision Agriculture: A Comprehensive Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21706v1)
- **Authors**: Saber Mehdipour, Seyed Abolghasem Mirroshandel, Seyed Amirhossein Tabatabaei
- **Abstract**: Detecting plant diseases is a crucial aspect of modern agriculture - it plays a key role in maintaining crop health and increasing overall yield. Traditional approaches, though still valuable, often rely on manual inspection or conventional machine learning techniques, both of which face limitations in scalability and accuracy. Recently, Vision Transformers (ViTs) have emerged as a promising alternative, offering benefits such as improved handling of long-range dependencies and better scalability for visual tasks. This survey explores the application of ViTs in precision agriculture, covering tasks from classification to detection and segmentation. We begin by introducing the foundational architecture of ViTs and discuss their transition from Natural Language Processing (NLP) to computer vision. The discussion includes the concept of inductive bias in traditional models like Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs), and how ViTs mitigate these biases. We provide a comprehensive review of recent literature, focusing on key methodologies, datasets, and performance metrics. The survey also includes a comparative analysis of CNNs and ViTs, with a look at hybrid models and performance enhancements. Technical challenges - such as data requirements, computational demands, and model interpretability - are addressed alongside potential solutions. Finally, we outline potential research directions and technological advancements that could further support the integration of ViTs in real-world agricultural settings. Our goal with this study is to offer practitioners and researchers a deeper understanding of how ViTs are poised to transform smart and precision agriculture.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided survey paper:

**Summary:**

The survey paper presents a comprehensive overview of Vision Transformers (ViTs) and their applications in precision agriculture, specifically focusing on plant disease detection. It begins by explaining the architecture of ViTs, their transition from Natural Language Processing (NLP) to computer vision, and the concept of inductive biases in comparison to Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs). The survey then delves into a review of recent literature, analyzing key methodologies, datasets, and performance metrics used in studies that apply ViTs to various agricultural tasks. It also provides a comparative analysis of CNNs and ViTs, addresses technical challenges like data requirements and computational demands, and outlines potential research directions. The paper aims to offer researchers and practitioners a deeper understanding of how ViTs can transform smart and precision agriculture.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper provides a detailed and well-structured overview of ViTs, including their foundational architecture and key components, and explains the transition from NLP to the computer vision domain.
    *   **Balanced Perspective:** The discussion of inductive biases is insightful, particularly the comparison between CNNs and ViTs. This helps readers understand the trade-offs between the two approaches.
    *   **Extensive Literature Review:** The survey covers a significant number of relevant studies (42 high-impact peer-reviewed papers) and categorizes the models effectively (pure vs. hybrid). The detailed summaries of these studies offer a solid foundation for readers interested in the field.
    *   **Identified Open Challenges:** The paper effectively highlights key challenges (data requirements, computational demands, interpretability) and suggests potential solutions, making it useful for guiding future research.
    *   **Clearly Defined Goals:** The stated aim is to offer a deep understanding of ViTs in agriculture, which the paper largely achieves.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Critical Analysis Beyond Summaries:** While the paper summarizes the studies, it lacks a deeper critical analysis of the *quality* of the experimental designs. Were the comparisons fair? Were the datasets truly representative? It could benefit from a more critical meta-analysis of the reported results.
    *   **Lack of Discussion of Economic Feasibility:** While the survey focuses on technical feasibility, a critical aspect often missing in academic research in agriculture is the economic feasibility of these advanced methods. A brief discussion of the cost-benefit analysis of deploying ViT-based solutions in real-world farming settings would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Novelty:** The paper is primarily a survey. While it synthesizes existing research well, it does not present any new algorithms, methodologies, or data.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper fills a gap by specifically focusing on ViTs in precision agriculture. While some reviews mention ViTs, they do not provide a focused analysis of this emerging area. To the best of the authors' knowledge, the review is the first that is solely focused on ViTs in precision agriculture.
    *   The paper contributes to the field by consolidating and categorizing the latest research, providing a structured overview that helps researchers navigate the growing literature on ViTs in agriculture.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper is likely to be a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners interested in applying ViTs to agricultural problems.
    *   The identification of open challenges could stimulate further research in addressing the limitations of ViTs and developing more practical and robust solutions.
    *   The comparison of CNNs and ViTs can help guide the selection of appropriate architectures for specific agricultural tasks.

*   **Room for Improvement**:

    *   The review could be improved by including more comparative analysis on the experiments and results reported in the referenced papers.
    *   It could be improved by discussing other transformer architectures and how they can be used in precision agriculture.
    *   It could be improved by more explicitly stating the current limitations of using ViTs in an agricultural setting.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper is a well-written, comprehensive survey that synthesizes the current state of research on ViTs in precision agriculture. It addresses a relevant and emerging topic, provides a clear explanation of the technical background, and identifies key challenges and future directions. However, it lacks a deeper critical analysis of the reviewed studies and does not present any novel contributions beyond the synthesis of existing knowledge. Therefore, while valuable, it does not represent a groundbreaking advance in the field.

### TheraQuest: A Gamified, LLM-Powered Simulation for Massage Therapy Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21735v1)
- **Authors**: Shengqian Wang
- **Abstract**: Massage therapy training emphasizes hands-on techniques and effective therapist--patient communication. However, many educational programs struggle to provide realistic practice scenarios. To address this problem, we propose TheraQuest, a gamified, web-based simulation platform that employs large language models (LLMs) to generate diverse virtual patients with varying symptoms and cultural backgrounds. Through interactive dialogue, anatomical decision-making, and immediate assessment, trainees develop both diagnostic reasoning and empathetic communication skills in a low-risk environment. Unlike exclusively VR-based solutions, TheraQuest remains accessible via standard web browsers, mitigating the cost and discomfort associated with extended headset use. Preliminary testing suggests that integrating LLM-driven virtual patients with real-time skill metrics can enhance trainee engagement and help bridge the gap between theoretical knowledge and clinical proficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TheraQuest, a gamified, web-based simulation platform designed to enhance massage therapy training. TheraQuest leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate diverse virtual patients with varying symptoms and cultural backgrounds. The platform aims to improve both diagnostic reasoning and empathetic communication skills through interactive dialogue, anatomical decision-making, and real-time assessment.  The key benefit highlighted is its accessibility via standard web browsers, offering a cost-effective alternative to VR-based solutions. The authors present the system's architecture, including features like therapy room customization, AI-driven patient interactions, and a skill-based assessment system. They also propose a two-stage evaluation strategy involving expert review and user studies to assess TheraQuest's effectiveness and realism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents a reasonable, yet not groundbreaking, level of novelty. While virtual patient simulations and gamification are not new concepts in medical education, their specific application to massage therapy training, coupled with the use of LLMs for dynamic patient generation, contributes a valuable, albeit incremental, advance to the field. The integration of web-based accessibility, offering a more affordable alternative to VR systems, also adds practical value.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a recognized gap in massage therapy training, particularly in developing communication skills and diagnostic reasoning in diverse patient populations. The potential to bridge the gap between theoretical knowledge and clinical proficiency is significant. By offering a low-risk environment for practice and feedback, TheraQuest could improve trainee confidence and competence.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper effectively identifies the challenges in traditional massage therapy training, particularly regarding patient communication and diagnostic reasoning.
    *   **Well-defined system architecture:** The description of TheraQuest's features, including AI-driven patient generation, interactive dialogue, and assessment metrics, provides a clear understanding of the system's capabilities.
    *   **Practical accessibility:** The focus on a web-based platform makes TheraQuest more accessible and cost-effective compared to VR-based alternatives.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation plan:** The proposed evaluation strategy, including expert review and user studies, demonstrates a commitment to rigorous assessment.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Lack of empirical results:** The paper presents a prototype but provides no empirical data to demonstrate its effectiveness. The proposed evaluation plan is promising, but the absence of preliminary findings weakens the claims.
    *   **Limited discussion of LLM training:** The paper mentions fine-tuning GPT models but lacks detail on the specific data used, the fine-tuning process, and potential biases. This is a critical area, as the realism and accuracy of the virtual patients depend heavily on the LLM's capabilities.
    *   **Limited discussion of tactile aspects:** Massage therapy is inherently a tactile practice. While the simulation focuses on communication and diagnosis, the lack of simulated tactile feedback is a limitation. The paper could address how it intends to mitigate this.
    *   **Overclaims:** The paper somewhat oversells the 'novelty' of the approach. While the specific combination of elements is unique, the individual components (virtual patients, LLMs, gamification) are well-established.

*   **Potential Impact:** If validated by empirical studies, TheraQuest could become a valuable tool for massage therapy educators. Its accessibility and potential to improve communication and diagnostic skills could contribute to better-prepared and more confident therapists. The framework also has the potential to be adapted for other healthcare disciplines.

In conclusion, TheraQuest is a promising platform with the potential to address key challenges in massage therapy training. However, the lack of empirical results and further details around the crucial LLM implementation limit its immediate impact. It requires strong validation to demonstrate its efficacy and practicality.

### Investigating Literary Motifs in Ancient and Medieval Novels with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21742v1)
- **Authors**: Emelie Hallenberg
- **Abstract**: The Greek fictional narratives often termed love novels or romances, ranging from the first century CE to the middle of the 15th century, have long been considered as similar in many ways, not least in the use of particular literary motifs. By applying the use of fine-tuned large language models, this study aims to investigate which motifs exactly that the texts in this corpus have in common, and in which ways they differ from each other. The results show that while some motifs persist throughout the corpus, others fluctuate in frequency, indicating certain trends or external influences. Conclusively, the method proves to adequately extract literary motifs according to a set definition, providing data for both quantitative and qualitative analyses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates literary motifs in a corpus of ancient and medieval Greek novels (love novels/romances) using fine-tuned large language models (LLMs).  The author aims to identify shared and distinct motifs across these texts spanning several centuries. The study extracts motifs using a fine-tuned GPT-4 model, embeds these motifs, clusters them with BERTopic, UMAP, and HDBSCAN, and then summarizes the clusters using Llama-3.  The paper analyzes the resulting data quantitatively, examining the frequency and persistence of motifs over time, and qualitatively, assessing the similarity between novels based on shared motifs. The results reveal both common and fluctuating motifs, offering insights into genre evolution and external influences. The author also discusses methodological challenges and emphasizes the importance of transparency and replicability in computational literary studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents an interesting application of LLMs to the study of literary motifs in a specific corpus of Greek novels.  However, the novelty and significance are somewhat limited by several factors:

*   **Methodological Novelty:** While using LLMs for motif extraction is a relatively recent approach in computational literary studies, the combination of fine-tuning, embedding, clustering, and summarizing is a fairly standard pipeline, drawing on well-established techniques (BERTopic, UMAP, HDBSCAN, Llama-3). The fine-tuning aspect is a plus, but it's unclear how extensive the impact of that training is on the core result (rather than just the readability of the output).

*   **Substantive Insights:**  The findings, while descriptive, do not offer radically new interpretations of the Greek novels. The observation that some motifs are persistent (fate, oaths) and others fluctuate (maiden's beauty, castles) aligns with existing scholarship. The emphasis on ekphrasis in Komnenian novels is known, and the inference of Western influence in Palaiologan romances, inferred from motifs like the king as character or the Eros as king motif, is also already debated in the field. That said, the paper offers an independent data driven confirmation of certain already circulating scholarly viewpoints, rather than formulating fresh ideas.

*   **Corpus Specificity:** The study focuses on a relatively well-defined and researched corpus. The insights are therefore largely specific to this body of work. The generalization of the methodology to other, less-studied corpora would significantly enhance the impact.

*   **LLM's Black Box:** A major weakness is the limited discussion of the LLM's "black box" nature.  The paper mentions the importance of transparency, but it doesn't delve deeply into *why* the LLM extracts particular motifs. How sensitive is the model to phrasing? How much does pre-training bias influence the results? How the model arrived at specific labels deserves more attention.

*   **Evaluation:** The evaluation is primarily manual, which is necessary, but more robust quantitative metrics for assessing the quality of the extracted motifs (e.g., comparing them to motifs identified by human experts) would be beneficial. The paper also relies heavily on relative frequency scores, with the author stating caution about the raw numbers - however, more justification for this, and exploration of alternate scoring, is needed.

*   **Contribution:** While the paper successfully demonstrates the extraction and analysis of literary motifs using LLMs, the relatively incremental nature of both the methodological and substantive contributions limits its overall impact. The biggest strength lies in demonstrating the *process* of using LLMs in this type of study, making a good case for the value and feasibility of a computationally aided approach.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides a useful demonstration of how LLMs can be applied to literary analysis. It does a solid job of extracting the motifs and putting them into understandable clusters - which itself is a valuable demonstration of the workflow that is relatively easy to understand. However, the findings are somewhat limited by the reliance on existing scholarship and the need for more justification behind using the scores they use. The paper's novelty is somewhat limited. While LLMs are new to this area, the process is not a breakthrough methodology and the results it provides do not make significant contributions to the field. Thus, a score of 5 is a fair and critical assessment of the paper's impact.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### LLM-based Interactive Imitation Learning for Robotic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21769v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Werner, Kun Chu, Cornelius Weber, Stefan Wermter
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in machine learning provide methods to train autonomous agents capable of handling the increasing complexity of sequential decision-making in robotics. Imitation Learning (IL) is a prominent approach, where agents learn to control robots based on human demonstrations. However, IL commonly suffers from violating the independent and identically distributed (i.i.d) assumption in robotic tasks. Interactive Imitation Learning (IIL) achieves improved performance by allowing agents to learn from interactive feedback from human teachers. Despite these improvements, both approaches come with significant costs due to the necessity of human involvement. Leveraging the emergent capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in reasoning and generating human-like responses, we introduce LLM-iTeach -- a novel IIL framework that utilizes an LLM as an interactive teacher to enhance agent performance while alleviating the dependence on human resources. Firstly, LLM-iTeach uses a hierarchical prompting strategy that guides the LLM in generating a policy in Python code. Then, with a designed similarity-based feedback mechanism, LLM-iTeach provides corrective and evaluative feedback interactively during the agent's training. We evaluate LLM-iTeach against baseline methods such as Behavior Cloning (BC), an IL method, and CEILing, a state-of-the-art IIL method using a human teacher, on various robotic manipulation tasks. Our results demonstrate that LLM-iTeach surpasses BC in the success rate and achieves or even outscores that of CEILing, highlighting the potential of LLMs as cost-effective, human-like teachers in interactive learning environments. We further demonstrate the method's potential for generalization by evaluating it on additional tasks. The code and prompts are provided at: https://github.com/Tubicor/LLM-iTeach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LLM-based Interactive Imitation Learning for Robotic Manipulation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-iTeach, a novel Interactive Imitation Learning (IIL) framework that utilizes a Large Language Model (LLM) as an interactive teacher for training robotic manipulation agents.  Instead of relying on human feedback, LLM-iTeach employs a hierarchical prompting strategy to generate a Python-based policy (CodePolicy) from the LLM. A similarity-based feedback mechanism is then used to provide corrective and evaluative feedback to the agent during training. The paper compares LLM-iTeach against Behavior Cloning (BC) and CEILing (a state-of-the-art IIL method with human teachers) on several robotic manipulation tasks. The results demonstrate that LLM-iTeach surpasses BC in success rate and either matches or exceeds CEILing's performance, suggesting the potential of LLMs as a cost-effective alternative to human teachers in IIL. The framework is also shown to be adaptable to new tasks through simple task descriptions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of replacing a human teacher with an LLM in an IIL setting is reasonably novel, though the application of LLMs to robotics is rapidly evolving. The hierarchical prompting strategy and the similarity-based feedback mechanism are also interesting design choices, but they build upon existing techniques like Chain-of-Thought prompting and code generation with LLMs. A key claim lies in the demonstration that LLMs can provide feedback and instructions just like human teachers. While the paper proposes a system architecture capable of integrating language and robotic actions, it is questionable if that makes it a fundamental innovation.

*   **Significance:** If the claims hold up under broader scrutiny, this work is reasonably significant because it tackles the cost and time constraints associated with human-in-the-loop IIL.  Automating the teaching process using LLMs makes IIL more scalable and accessible. The paper demonstrates comparable or superior performance compared to CEILing in some tasks, indicating that LLMs can indeed act as effective teachers in certain contexts. It is unclear whether this is enough to be a "paradigm-shifting" innovation for the field. In particular, CEILing is a recent state of the art but has not been widely applied, which reduces the impact somewhat.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of traditional IIL (reliance on human teachers) and proposes a solution based on LLMs.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** The LLM-iTeach framework is well-defined, with a clear explanation of the hierarchical prompting strategy, the feedback mechanism, and the agent's learning process.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive empirical evaluation, comparing LLM-iTeach against BC and CEILing on multiple robotic manipulation tasks.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of different components of the framework (e.g., corrective vs. evaluative feedback).
    *   **Extendability:** The demonstration of the framework's applicability to additional tasks is a strong point, showcasing its potential for generalization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited LLM Reasoning and Robotic Awareness:** The LLM teacher's reasoning is inherently limited by its lack of real-world experience. The paper acknowledges that the LLM struggles with tasks involving physical constraints (e.g., how to close a microwave door), highlighting the need for better grounding of LLMs in the physical world.
    *   **Dependence on API and Simulated Environment:** The framework relies on an API for object positions and robot states, as well as the simulation environment of RLBench. This raises questions about its applicability to real-world robotic systems, where perception and control are more challenging.
    *   **Incremental Nature:** While the integration of LLMs is useful, the paper's core techniques (hierarchical prompting, similarity-based feedback) are adaptations of existing methods. The IIL training loop is similar to other methods like CEILing.
    *   **Warm-Start Policy:** The paper states a warm-start is used, but this doesn't fully solve the initial exploration problems that make robot learning difficult to converge on.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence future research in IIL and robotic learning by demonstrating the feasibility of using LLMs as cost-effective teachers. It could inspire the development of more sophisticated LLM-based IIL frameworks that address the limitations of the current approach (e.g., better grounding in the physical world, more robust perception and control). It may influence how robotics researchers view the applicability of LLMs. The easy extensibility of the framework to new tasks also suggests that it could be used as a platform for exploring different teaching strategies and learning algorithms.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel application of LLMs to address a significant challenge in IIL. However, the novelty is incremental, building on existing techniques in LLM prompting and IIL. The limitations related to LLM reasoning, dependence on simulated environments, and the need for external APIs are also important considerations. The impact on the field is somewhat limited due to its dependence on a simulation environment and warm-start policy. I think a more solid comparison to CEILing would have been good. A stronger approach to initial exploration beyond warm-starts would also have been compelling.

### LASHED: LLMs And Static Hardware Analysis for Early Detection of RTL Bugs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21770v1)
- **Authors**: Baleegh Ahmad, Hammond Pearce, Ramesh Karri, Benjamin Tan
- **Abstract**: While static analysis is useful in detecting early-stage hardware security bugs, its efficacy is limited because it requires information to form checks and is often unable to explain the security impact of a detected vulnerability. Large Language Models can be useful in filling these gaps by identifying relevant assets, removing false violations flagged by static analysis tools, and explaining the reported violations. LASHED combines the two approaches (LLMs and Static Analysis) to overcome each other's limitations for hardware security bug detection. We investigate our approach on four open-source SoCs for five Common Weakness Enumerations (CWEs) and present strategies for improvement with better prompt engineering. We find that 87.5% of instances flagged by our recommended scheme are plausible CWEs. In-context learning and asking the model to 'think again' improves LASHED's precision.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LASHED, a novel framework that combines Large Language Models (LLMs) and static analysis techniques to detect security vulnerabilities in Register Transfer Level (RTL) hardware code. LASHED uses LLMs to identify security-relevant assets, remove false positives from static analysis results (linting or formal verification), and explain the security implications of detected violations based on Common Weakness Enumerations (CWEs).  The authors evaluate LASHED on four open-source RISC-V SoCs, targeting five specific hardware CWEs, and explore different prompt engineering strategies to enhance the LLM's precision.  They find that combining LLMs and static analysis improves bug detection and that prompt engineering, particularly asking the model to "think again," increases the framework's accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its novel integration of LLMs with traditional static analysis methods for hardware security vulnerability detection at the RTL level. While LLMs and static analysis have individually been applied to hardware and software security, the specific combination and workflow presented in LASHED, particularly the LLM-guided asset identification and contextualization of static analysis results, is a novel contribution. Existing works mainly apply LLMs for code generation or automated debugging. It's not *entirely* groundbreaking, as similar approaches exist in software security, but the adaptation to hardware, specifically RTL, is a definite advancement.

*   **Significance:** Early-stage security bug detection in hardware is a crucial area. The high cost of fixing vulnerabilities late in the design cycle justifies exploring novel methods. LASHED addresses two key limitations of traditional static analysis: the need for pre-defined rules and the lack of context/explanation for identified violations. LLMs can help overcome these limitations. The ability to identify relevant assets and reason about potential vulnerabilities is a valuable capability. While the experimental results show promising precision, the modest recall limits the immediate practical significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenge of early-stage hardware security bug detection and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** LASHED is presented as a well-structured framework with clearly defined steps (Asset Identification, Static Analysis, Contextualization).
    *   **Experimental Evaluation:** The evaluation on four open-source SoCs, covering five CWEs, provides empirical evidence of the framework's capabilities and limitations.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Analysis:**  The investigation of different prompt strategies is valuable for understanding how to effectively leverage LLMs in this context.
    *   **Addresses False Positives:** The most significant contribution is the approach taken to reduce the number of false positives that result from static analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:**  The evaluation focuses on a relatively small set of CWEs and SoCs.  Generalizability to other types of vulnerabilities and more complex designs remains to be proven.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The reliance on LLMs introduces uncertainties. The LLM's performance is sensitive to prompt engineering, and results can vary based on the specific LLM used. A more comprehensive analysis of LLM stability and robustness is needed.
    *   **Recall Concerns:** While precision is reasonably good with improved prompting, the paper does not address recall. How many actual bugs does it miss? This is important. Also, the evaluation does not establish true *ground truth* for security bugs, relying instead on the researchers' assessment. This could introduce bias.
    *   **Manual Evaluation:**  The manual evaluation of flagged violations is a potential bottleneck and source of error. Automating this process would improve the scalability and objectivity of the framework.
    *   **Lack of comparison with previous work** LASHED could have benefitted from a better comparison with other approaches.

*   **Impact:** LASHED has the potential to influence the field of hardware security by demonstrating the benefits of combining LLMs and static analysis for vulnerability detection. It provides a foundation for future research exploring more sophisticated LLM-based techniques and automated verification workflows. However, the scalability and generalizability limitations currently restrict its widespread adoption.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to an important problem. The experimental results provide a good foundation for future improvements. The framework is well-defined, and the prompt engineering analysis is insightful. However, the limited scope of the evaluation, recall concerns, and dependency on LLMs restrict its immediate impact. A more rigorous recall evaluation and demonstration on a wider range of CWEs/SoCs would significantly increase the paper's significance. Therefore, a score in the middle of the spectrum is most appropriate.

### MAC-Tuning: LLM Multi-Compositional Problem Reasoning with Enhanced Knowledge Boundary Awareness
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21773v1)
- **Authors**: Junsheng Huang, Zhitao He, Sandeep Polisetty, Qingyun Wang, May Fung
- **Abstract**: With the widespread application of large language models (LLMs), the issue of generating non-existing facts, known as hallucination, has garnered increasing attention. Previous research in enhancing LLM confidence estimation mainly focuses on the single problem setting. However, LLM awareness of its internal parameterized knowledge boundary under the more challenging multi-problem setting, which requires answering multiple problems accurately simultaneously, remains underexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce a novel method, Multiple Answers and Confidence Stepwise Tuning (MAC-Tuning), that separates the learning of answer prediction and confidence estimation during fine-tuning on instruction data. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms baselines by up to 25% in average precision.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MAC-Tuning, a novel fine-tuning method designed to improve the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to reason about and answer multiple related questions simultaneously while maintaining awareness of their knowledge boundaries.  MAC-Tuning addresses the issue of LLM hallucination in the challenging "multi-problem setting," where a single input contains multiple sub-questions. The method separates the learning of answer prediction from confidence estimation during fine-tuning using automatically labeled data that indicates whether the model is certain or uncertain about its answer. The paper presents experimental results across various datasets demonstrating that MAC-Tuning outperforms baseline methods in Average Precision (AP) and achieves better calibrated confidence scores.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of separating answer prediction and confidence estimation during fine-tuning, particularly in the context of a multi-problem setting, demonstrates considerable novelty. Existing work has primarily focused on either single-problem hallucination mitigation or multi-task learning without specifically addressing confidence calibration for multiple related questions. The automated method for labeling certainty/uncertainty also has value and distinguishes it from approaches that rely on manual annotation or heuristics.
*   **Significance:** Hallucination is a critical limitation of LLMs, directly impacting their reliability and trustworthiness. The multi-problem setting represents a realistic usage scenario where LLMs are expected to process complex inputs and synthesize information from multiple sources. By enhancing confidence estimation in this context, the paper addresses a practical problem. The performance gains demonstrated in the experiments, specifically the improvements in AP and ECE, support the significance of the contribution. Also the paper studies various settings and datasets which increases its significance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the challenges of the multi-problem setting and motivates the need for improved confidence calibration.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** MAC-Tuning is presented as a straightforward and implementable approach with the use of an automated labeling process.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are comprehensive, spanning multiple datasets and comparing against relevant baselines. The ablation studies further dissect the impact of different components of MAC-Tuning.
    *   **Consistent Results:** The performance improvements are consistent across a variety of datasets. The study related to the different base models further strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Confidence Labels:** The confidence labels are binary ("I am sure" or "I am unsure").  More granular confidence levels or probabilities could potentially lead to further improvements.
    *   **Dataset selection**: It is better to select an equal amount of QA datasets from different contexts instead of choosing only SQA for this task, which may cause a bias towards one of the domains.
    *   **Lack of In-Depth Error Analysis:** While the quantitative results are presented thoroughly, the paper would benefit from a more detailed qualitative error analysis. Understanding the types of mistakes MAC-Tuning still makes could provide further insights and drive future research.
*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM fine-tuning and hallucination mitigation. The emphasis on confidence calibration in multi-problem scenarios could encourage researchers to explore more sophisticated methods for improving LLM reliability. Additionally, the paper raises interesting questions about the trade-offs between accuracy and certainty, which could drive new directions in LLM research.

**Overall:** The paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing a critical problem in a realistic usage scenario for LLMs. The proposed method is novel and well-validated by empirical evidence. While there are some limitations and areas for improvement, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper offers a novel approach to a relevant and timely problem. The experimental results provide compelling evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. The identified weaknesses, such as the binary confidence labels and the need for more detailed error analysis, are opportunities for future work. This provides a solid foundation for further research and development in LLM calibration and hallucination mitigation in complex reasoning scenarios.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### DeepSeek-Prover-V2: Advancing Formal Mathematical Reasoning via Reinforcement Learning for Subgoal Decomposition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21801v1)
- **Authors**: Z. Z. Ren, Zhihong Shao, Junxiao Song, Huajian Xin, Haocheng Wang, Wanjia Zhao, Liyue Zhang, Zhe Fu, Qihao Zhu, Dejian Yang, Z. F. Wu, Zhibin Gou, Shirong Ma, Hongxuan Tang, Yuxuan Liu, Wenjun Gao, Daya Guo, Chong Ruan
- **Abstract**: We introduce DeepSeek-Prover-V2, an open-source large language model designed for formal theorem proving in Lean 4, with initialization data collected through a recursive theorem proving pipeline powered by DeepSeek-V3. The cold-start training procedure begins by prompting DeepSeek-V3 to decompose complex problems into a series of subgoals. The proofs of resolved subgoals are synthesized into a chain-of-thought process, combined with DeepSeek-V3's step-by-step reasoning, to create an initial cold start for reinforcement learning. This process enables us to integrate both informal and formal mathematical reasoning into a unified model. The resulting model, DeepSeek-Prover-V2-671B, achieves state-of-the-art performance in neural theorem proving, reaching 88.9% pass ratio on the MiniF2F-test and solving 49 out of 658 problems from PutnamBench. In addition to standard benchmarks, we introduce ProverBench, a collection of 325 formalized problems, to enrich our evaluation, including 15 selected problems from the recent AIME competitions (years 24-25). Further evaluation on these 15 AIME problems shows that the model successfully solves 6 of them. In comparison, DeepSeek-V3 solves 8 of these problems using majority voting, highlighting that the gap between formal and informal mathematical reasoning in large language models is substantially narrowing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the DeepSeek-Prover-V2 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepSeek-Prover-V2, an open-source large language model (LLM) specialized for formal theorem proving in Lean 4. A key aspect is the recursive theorem proving pipeline, which leverages DeepSeek-V3 for both subgoal decomposition and proof formalization. The training procedure involves a cold-start phase where DeepSeek-V3 decomposes complex problems and creates proof sketches, followed by recursively solving subgoals with a smaller 7B model. Reinforcement learning further refines the model's capabilities. The resulting DeepSeek-Prover-V2-671B model achieves state-of-the-art results on various benchmarks, including MiniF2F-test, PutnamBench, and a newly introduced ProverBench. The paper emphasizes the narrowing gap between informal mathematical reasoning and formal verification systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a combination of techniques that, while individually perhaps not groundbreaking, together constitute a notable advance in neural theorem proving. The recursive decomposition approach is reminiscent of prior work, but the integration of a strong general-purpose LLM (DeepSeek-V3) for *both* proof sketching and formalization is significant. The curriculum learning method, leveraging generated subgoals, is a valuable addition. The creation of the ProverBench dataset is also a useful contribution to the community, particularly including AIME problems. The data synthesis strategy, using a more general purpose model to decompose the problem as well as provide chain-of-thought, is interesting and potentially more effective than other methods.
*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates substantial progress in formal theorem proving. The performance improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods on established benchmarks like MiniF2F and PutnamBench are impressive. Moreover, the success on AIME problems, while modest (6 out of 15), showcases a potential for LLMs to tackle competition-level problems in a formal setting, a domain where they have previously struggled.  The narrowing of the gap between informal and formal mathematical reasoning is a promising sign for the future of AI-assisted mathematics. The model's ability to solve previously intractable problems, even at a high computation cost, highlights the potential of the approach.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with detailed descriptions of the training procedure, data collection, and evaluation setup.
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks.
    *   Introduction of a new benchmark dataset (ProverBench).
    *   Careful analysis of results and comparison with existing methods.
    *   Open-source release of the model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational cost: The reliance on a large 671B parameter model and extensive sampling during inference may limit its accessibility and wider adoption. This issue can be mitigated by the release of a smaller model.
    *   Limited success on AIME problems: While the results are promising, solving only 6 out of 15 problems indicates room for further improvement. It needs to be clarified, however, that these are harder problems that previous models have failed to solve.
    *   Incremental contribution:  While the overall combination of techniques is impressive, the individual components (recursive proving, reinforcement learning) have precedents in prior work.
    *   The reliance on a proprietary model (DeepSeek-V3) in the data generation pipeline presents a barrier to full reproducibility and future research that seeks to explore alternative data synthesis strategies. Although the end result is an open-source model, the crucial cold-start data generation relies on a closed model.
    *   The experiments are not sufficiently motivated as to *why* each method was chosen. While it is impressive that the model is state-of-the-art, it is not explained exactly why the improvements are so significant, or why those methods work. The most that it offers is that the method "is promising" without giving good justification.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of neural theorem proving. The open-source release of DeepSeek-Prover-V2 will facilitate further research and development. The proposed methodology, particularly the combination of subgoal decomposition and reinforcement learning, could serve as a foundation for future work. The new ProverBench dataset will provide a valuable resource for evaluating and comparing different approaches.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

DeepSeek-Prover-V2 represents a solid step forward in neural theorem proving by demonstrating excellent results. The combination of techniques, particularly the reliance on DeepSeek-V3, presents some challenges. The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary, but the overall system is well-engineered and delivers tangible improvements, with the creation of the ProverBench dataset a great benefit.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### An Empirical Study on the Effectiveness of Large Language Models for Binary Code Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21803v1)
- **Authors**: Xiuwei Shang, Zhenkan Fu, Shaoyin Cheng, Guoqiang Chen, Gangyang Li, Li Hu, Weiming Zhang, Nenghai Yu
- **Abstract**: Binary code analysis plays a pivotal role in the field of software security and is widely used in tasks such as software maintenance, malware detection, software vulnerability discovery, patch analysis, etc. However, unlike source code, reverse engineers face significant challenges in understanding binary code due to the lack of intuitive semantic information. Although traditional reverse tools can convert binary code into C-like pseudo code, the lack of code comments and symbolic information such as function names still makes code understanding difficult. In recent years, two groups of techniques have shown promising prospects: (1) Deep learning-based techniques have demonstrated competitive results in tasks related to binary code understanding, furthermore, (2) Large Language Models (LLMs) have been extensively pre-trained at the source-code level for tasks such as code understanding and generation. This has left participants wondering about the capabilities of LLMs in binary code understanding. To this end, this work proposes a benchmark to evaluate the effectiveness of LLMs in real-world reverse engineering scenarios, which covers two key binary code understanding tasks, i.e., function name recovery and binary code summarization. To more comprehensively evaluate, we include binaries with multiple target architectures as well as different optimization options. We gain valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations through extensive empirical studies of popular LLMs using our benchmark. Our evaluations reveal that existing LLMs can understand binary code to a certain extent, thereby improving the efficiency of binary code analysis. Our results highlight the great potential of the LLMs in advancing the field of binary code understanding, and provide new directions for binary code analysis techniques.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the OCR output.

**Summary:**

The paper, titled "An Empirical Study on the Effectiveness of Large Language Models for Binary Code Understanding," investigates the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in binary code analysis.  The authors created a benchmark dataset of aligned source code, decompiled pseudo-code, and natural language summaries to evaluate LLMs on two key binary code understanding tasks: function name recovery and binary code summarization. They tested several popular code-domain and general-domain LLMs, comparing their performance against deep learning-based expert models. They also explored the impact of different target architectures, optimization levels, few-shot prompting, and fine-tuning on LLM performance. The results indicate that LLMs show promise in understanding binary code, with models like CodeLlama and ChatGPT achieving notable results. However, challenges remain, particularly with complex code structures and robustness to obfuscation. Fine-tuning with binary domain knowledge significantly improves performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive empirical evaluation of LLMs for binary code understanding using a custom-built benchmark. While prior work has explored deep learning for binary code analysis and the general capabilities of LLMs for code, this paper directly addresses the question of *how effectively* general and specifically-trained LLMs perform on core reverse engineering tasks. The exploration of different architectures, optimization levels, and prompt engineering techniques adds to the depth of the study. However, the core tasks themselves (function name recovery and summarization) are not entirely new.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it provides valuable insights into the potential and limitations of applying LLMs to a challenging domain like binary code analysis. Binary analysis is crucial for software security, vulnerability detection, and malware analysis. The findings can guide future research directions in this area, suggesting how to best leverage and adapt LLMs for reverse engineering tasks. The case studies demonstrate the practical potential of these models in assisting reverse engineers. The public release of the benchmark also enhances the research contributions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation using a diverse set of LLMs, target architectures, and compiler optimization levels.
    *   **Benchmark Dataset:** The creation and public release of a benchmark dataset aligned in multiple ways is a strong contribution, enabling further research and comparison.
    *   **Analysis of Key Factors:** The study delves into factors influencing LLM performance, like context window size, few-shot prompting, and fine-tuning, providing practical guidance.
    *   **Case Studies:** The inclusion of real-world virus analysis case studies helps illustrate the practical potential of LLMs in assisting reverse engineers.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The paper relies primarily on traditional text generation metrics (BLEU, ROUGE, METEOR) for code summarization, which might not fully capture the nuances of reverse engineering expertise. A metric considering key code elements or code functionality is preferable, which the paper identifies as a limitation in Section 5.2.
    *   **Limited Scope of Obfuscation:** The evaluation does not address the robustness of LLMs to code obfuscation, a common technique used in malware to hinder analysis. Section 5.2 identifies binary code obfuscation as a limitation.
    *   **Dependence on Decompilation:** The reliance on decompiled pseudo-code as input may limit the evaluation, as the quality of decompilation can significantly impact the effectiveness of LLMs.
    *   **Generalization:** It is worth noting that the conclusions are somewhat limited by the specific benchmark dataset used. Results may vary on different codebases.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research by establishing a baseline for LLM-based binary code analysis and highlighting promising directions for improvement. It may also spur the development of more specialized LLMs and tools for reverse engineering.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper offers a valuable contribution to the field by systematically evaluating LLMs for binary code understanding and releasing a novel benchmark, it also faces limitations. The core idea of applying LLMs to this task isn't entirely novel, and the reliance on traditional metrics and the absence of obfuscation analysis are noticeable weaknesses.
Given the comprehensiveness of the study, the valuable benchmark creation, the insights gained, the public availability of this dataset, and its impact on the area of applying LLMs to binary code analysis, but considering the weaknesses identified, I would assign this paper a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Why Compress What You Can Generate? When GPT-4o Generation Ushers in Image Compression Fields
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21814v1)
- **Authors**: Yixin Gao, Xiaohan Pan, Xin Li, Zhibo Chen
- **Abstract**: The rapid development of AIGC foundation models has revolutionized the paradigm of image compression, which paves the way for the abandonment of most pixel-level transform and coding, compelling us to ask: why compress what you can generate if the AIGC foundation model is powerful enough to faithfully generate intricate structure and fine-grained details from nothing more than some compact descriptors, i.e., texts, or cues. Fortunately, recent GPT-4o image generation of OpenAI has achieved impressive cross-modality generation, editing, and design capabilities, which motivates us to answer the above question by exploring its potential in image compression fields. In this work, we investigate two typical compression paradigms: textual coding and multimodal coding (i.e., text + extremely low-resolution image), where all/most pixel-level information is generated instead of compressing via the advanced GPT-4o image generation function. The essential challenge lies in how to maintain semantic and structure consistency during the decoding process. To overcome this, we propose a structure raster-scan prompt engineering mechanism to transform the image into textual space, which is compressed as the condition of GPT-4o image generation. Extensive experiments have shown that the combination of our designed structural raster-scan prompts and GPT-4o's image generation function achieved the impressive performance compared with recent multimodal/generative image compression at ultra-low bitrate, further indicating the potential of AIGC generation in image compression fields.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of using the OpenAI GPT-4o model for image compression, specifically at ultra-low bitrates.  Instead of traditional pixel-wise coding, the authors investigate generating the image using GPT-4o conditioned on either a text description (textual coding) or a combination of text and an extremely low-resolution image (multimodal coding). A key contribution is a "structural raster-scan prompt engineering" method to generate text descriptions that guide the GPT-4o image generation process, preserving both perceptual quality and consistency with the original image.  Experiments demonstrate that the proposed method achieves competitive performance compared to existing generative compression approaches without requiring additional training of the GPT-4o model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of leveraging AIGC models *directly* (without fine-tuning or complex modifications) for image compression is relatively novel, though several works have explored generative codecs. The proposed structured raster-scan prompt engineering is a practical contribution to adapting AIGC for image compression and enhancing consistency. The paper's main novelty lies in the specific approach of using GPT-4o and the crafted prompting strategy. However, the general concept of generating images from descriptions for compression is not brand new.

*   **Significance:** Image compression, especially at ultra-low bitrates, is a crucial area with significant practical applications. Demonstrating that AIGC models can be effectively used for this purpose *without extensive training* could lead to simplified compression pipelines and potentially improved perceptual quality compared to traditional or fine-tuned generative methods. The paper convincingly shows GPT-4o's potential, which is significant. However, the dependence on a proprietary model like GPT-4o limits widespread adoption and reproducibility in its current form. Also, while the results are "competitive," they don't necessarily represent a *breakthrough* level of performance, suggesting room for future work in improving the prompting strategy and the use of other conditional information.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-written presentation.
    *   Addresses a relevant and important problem.
    *   Provides a practical method (raster-scan prompting) for leveraging GPT-4o.
    *   Experiments show promising results, demonstrating the potential of the approach.
    *   Does not require training or fine-tuning of a specific model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relies on a proprietary (and potentially unstable) model:  GPT-4o. The method's future viability depends on OpenAI's continued support and accessibility.
    *   Limited generalizability of the specific prompting strategy. The effectiveness of the prompt engineering may be dataset-dependent.
    *   While competitive, the performance gains compared to existing state-of-the-art methods are not overwhelmingly significant.
    *   The focus on DIV2K, while standard, is still a limited dataset. More diverse datasets would strengthen the evaluation.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could inspire further research on AIGC-based image compression methods, specifically focusing on how to best condition generative models using text and other compact representations. It could also prompt investigations into more robust and adaptable prompting techniques and the use of open-source generative models that can replicate the results of GPT-4o.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty (incremental but valuable), significance (relevant problem with potential for practical impact), the reliance on a proprietary model (limiting widespread adoption), and the level of performance gain (competitive, but not revolutionary), a score of 6 seems appropriate. The paper presents a solid contribution to the field, highlighting a promising new direction, but the limitations associated with GPT-4o and the need for further improvements in performance and robustness prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### 3D Stylization via Large Reconstruction Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21836v1)
- **Authors**: Ipek Oztas, Duygu Ceylan, Aysegul Dundar
- **Abstract**: With the growing success of text or image guided 3D generators, users demand more control over the generation process, appearance stylization being one of them. Given a reference image, this requires adapting the appearance of a generated 3D asset to reflect the visual style of the reference while maintaining visual consistency from multiple viewpoints. To tackle this problem, we draw inspiration from the success of 2D stylization methods that leverage the attention mechanisms in large image generation models to capture and transfer visual style. In particular, we probe if large reconstruction models, commonly used in the context of 3D generation, has a similar capability. We discover that the certain attention blocks in these models capture the appearance specific features. By injecting features from a visual style image to such blocks, we develop a simple yet effective 3D appearance stylization method. Our method does not require training or test time optimization. Through both quantitative and qualitative evaluations, we demonstrate that our approach achieves superior results in terms of 3D appearance stylization, significantly improving efficiency while maintaining high-quality visual outcomes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "3D Stylization via Large Reconstruction Model" presents a novel method for stylizing 3D objects using pre-trained large reconstruction models (LRMs).  It leverages the attention mechanisms within these models, specifically identifying that later attention blocks capture appearance-specific features. The key idea is to inject features from a style image into these attention blocks, enabling 3D appearance stylization without requiring any training or per-object optimization.  The authors demonstrate that by blending the attention outputs from the original multi-view images and the style image, they achieve high-quality stylization while preserving the underlying 3D geometry.  The method is evaluated both quantitatively and qualitatively, showing superior performance compared to existing 2D and 3D stylization techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its exploitation of existing, pre-trained large reconstruction models for a task (3D stylization) beyond their original purpose (3D reconstruction). This "zero-shot" adaptation is significant. While 3D stylization is not a new problem, the approach of leveraging attention mechanisms within an LRM *without* fine-tuning is a clear differentiator. The discovery that specific attention blocks within the LRM architecture implicitly encode appearance information is also a notable contribution.  The adaptation of 2D attention manipulation techniques to 3D reconstruction models provides an innovative synthesis of approaches. However, the inspiration from previous 2D stylization techniques that manipulate attention mechanisms to transfer style somewhat reduces the novelty of the core idea.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the method's efficiency and practicality.  Eliminating the need for per-object optimization or training makes it suitable for interactive 3D generation pipelines, a substantial improvement over optimization-based techniques like StyleRF or ARF.  The demonstrated performance improvement in style fidelity and comparable content fidelity over various baselines is compelling. The paper addresses a significant user need: more control over the 3D generation process, and specifically the ability to easily apply artistic styles. This has a direct impact on the usability and creative potential of 3D generation tools. The thorough experimental evaluation, including a user study, strengthens the claims and increases the paper's impact. The exploration of the effect of varying the injection layer shows an understanding of how to modify parameters to have maximum effect on the outcome.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Efficient and Practical: Training-free and no per-object optimization required.
    *   Effective Stylization: Demonstrates superior style fidelity.
    *   Content Preservation: Maintains 3D geometry reasonably well.
    *   Thorough Evaluation: Extensive quantitative and qualitative comparisons.
    *   Novel Approach: Adaption of 2D attention manipulation techniques to LRMs for 3D stylization.
    *   Solid Justification: Experiments for key design choices (number of layers) are clearly articulated

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on InstantMesh: The method's performance is inherently tied to the underlying reconstruction quality of InstantMesh.  Future improvements in LRMs could further enhance the results, but the current state limits the maximum achievable quality.
    *   Geometry Limitation: Geometry is mostly kept intact; further exploration of stylistic geometry changes as well is required.
    *   Some Intricate detail loss: Method results in abstraction or simplification of finer detail.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in 3D content creation, specifically in the areas of controllable generation and stylization. It opens up new avenues for exploring the capabilities of pre-trained models and for adapting techniques from 2D image processing to 3D graphics. The efficiency of the method may encourage the development of real-time 3D stylization tools.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of 3D content generation and stylization. The novel approach of leveraging pre-trained LRMs for style transfer *without* training is a significant advantage, leading to increased efficiency and practicality. However, some of the novelty is borrowed from prior work done in the 2D domain, and the results are also tied to the performance of the underlying LRM. Taking all this into consideration, the paper deserves a score that reflects a strong but not groundbreaking contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### COMPACT: COMPositional Atomic-to-Complex Visual Capability Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21850v1)
- **Authors**: Xindi Wu, Hee Seung Hwang, Polina Kirichenko, Olga Russakovsky
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at simple vision-language tasks but struggle when faced with complex tasks that require multiple capabilities, such as simultaneously recognizing objects, counting them, and understanding their spatial relationships. This might be partially the result of the fact that Visual Instruction Tuning (VIT), a critical training step for MLLMs, has traditionally focused on scaling data volume, but not the compositional complexity of training examples. We propose COMPACT (COMPositional Atomic-to-complex visual Capability Tuning), which generates a training dataset explicitly controlling for the compositional complexity of the training examples. The data from COMPACT allows MLLMs to train on combinations of atomic capabilities to learn complex capabilities more efficiently. Across all benchmarks, COMPACT achieves comparable performance to the LLaVA-665k VIT while using less than 10% of its data budget, and even outperforms it on several, especially those involving complex multi-capability tasks. For example, COMPACT achieves substantial 83.3% improvement on MMStar and 94.0% improvement on MM-Vet compared to the full-scale VIT on particularly complex questions that require four or more atomic capabilities. COMPACT offers a scalable, data-efficient, visual compositional tuning recipe to improve on complex visual-language tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "COMPACT: COMPositional Atomic-to-Complex Visual Capability Tuning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces COMPACT, a novel visual instruction tuning (VIT) recipe designed to improve the compositional capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Instead of simply scaling the volume of VIT data, COMPACT focuses on systematically controlling and balancing the compositional complexity of training examples. It decomposes complex visual tasks into combinations of atomic visual capabilities (e.g., object recognition, color attribution, spatial reasoning). A training dataset is then generated that explicitly covers varying levels of compositional complexity (k=1, 2, 3), ensuring the MLLM is exposed to diverse combinations of atomic capabilities.  The method combines this compositionally structured data with a small subset of a conventional VIT dataset (LLaVA-665K). Experiments show that COMPACT achieves comparable or better performance than full-scale VIT, particularly on tasks requiring multiple capabilities, while using significantly less data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the explicit modeling and control of compositional complexity within the VIT training process.  Traditional VIT approaches primarily rely on data scaling, hoping that compositional understanding will emerge implicitly. COMPACT, however, directly addresses the *lack* of compositional diversity in existing VIT datasets by creating a specialized training set designed to build complex capabilities from simpler, atomic ones. The systematic breakdown of visual tasks into atomic capabilities is also valuable. This departs from the common practice of data scaling, focusing on a data-efficient and principled approach.

*   **Significance:** The significance is multi-fold:
    *   **Data Efficiency:** Demonstrating comparable or superior performance with a fraction of the data used in full-scale VIT is highly significant. This reduces the compute and resource requirements associated with training MLLMs.
    *   **Improved Generalization:**  The ability to generalize to higher compositional complexity tasks (k>3) without explicit training on such data points demonstrates a valuable capacity of the model.
    *   **Understanding Compositionality:**  The ablations and analyses provide valuable insights into the factors that contribute to compositional understanding, such as the distribution of complexities and the coverage of atomic capabilities. This helps the field to move beyond simply scaling data, and towards understanding *how* models learn to compose skills.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:**  The paper presents a well-defined and systematic data generation and training pipeline.  The different components are clearly described.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The experiments are performed on a wide range of benchmarks. The ablations and analyses are thorough, providing insights into the method's effectiveness.
    *   **Clear Results:**  The results convincingly demonstrate the benefits of COMPACT.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Closed-Source Models for Data Generation:** The data generation pipeline depends on Gemini-2.0-Flash, a closed-source model, which makes the approach less reproducible and potentially introduces biases from the pre-trained model into the training data.
    *   **Limited Compositional Complexity:** While addressing a key limitation, the method focuses on relatively low levels of compositional complexity (k <= 3), due to the limitations of using LLMs to generate training questions with higher levels of complexity.
    *   **Limited improvements on Knowledge-Intensive Tasks:** While not the paper's focus, the comparatively limited improvement on knowledge-intensive tasks suggests areas for future research. A discussion of these limitations is included.

*   **Potential Impact:** COMPACT offers a promising direction for improving the compositional capabilities of MLLMs in a data-efficient manner. Its potential impact includes making MLLM training more accessible and fostering a better understanding of how to build compositionality into these models. The insights from the ablations and analyses can guide future research in this area.

*  **Areas to expand:** Future studies could expand the atomic visual capabilities, such as including an additional category for "causal reasoning". The capabilities for knowledge intensive tasks could be improved with "world knowledge".

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper is a significant contribution to the field because it proposes a novel and data-efficient approach to improve the compositional capabilities of MLLMs, an area of ongoing challenges for these models. The method is well-designed, rigorously evaluated, and provides valuable insights into the factors that influence compositional understanding. The paper's strength is its focus on building these skills with explicit data structuring, not just brute force scaling. The primary limitation is its reliance on closed-source data generation. The reliance on a black-box LLM to generate questions introduces a dependency on a non-reproducible process, a bias that may be hard to control, and limited compositonal complexity due to the LLMs limitations. Overall, it represents a valuable advance with promising potential to shape future research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### TRUST: An LLM-Based Dialogue System for Trauma Understanding and Structured Assessments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21851v1)
- **Authors**: Sichang Tu, Abigail Powers, Stephen Doogan, Jinho D. Choi
- **Abstract**: Objectives: While Large Language Models (LLMs) have been widely used to assist clinicians and support patients, no existing work has explored dialogue systems for standard diagnostic interviews and assessments. This study aims to bridge the gap in mental healthcare accessibility by developing an LLM-powered dialogue system that replicates clinician behavior. Materials and Methods: We introduce TRUST, a framework of cooperative LLM modules capable of conducting formal diagnostic interviews and assessments for Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder (PTSD). To guide the generation of appropriate clinical responses, we propose a Dialogue Acts schema specifically designed for clinical interviews. Additionally, we develop a patient simulation approach based on real-life interview transcripts to replace time-consuming and costly manual testing by clinicians. Results: A comprehensive set of evaluation metrics is designed to assess the dialogue system from both the agent and patient simulation perspectives. Expert evaluations by conversation and clinical specialists show that TRUST performs comparably to real-life clinical interviews. Discussion: Our system performs at the level of average clinicians, with room for future enhancements in communication styles and response appropriateness. Conclusions: Our TRUST framework shows its potential to facilitate mental healthcare availability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TRUST: An LLM-Based Dialogue System for Trauma Understanding and Structured Assessments":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TRUST, a novel LLM-powered dialogue system designed to conduct formal diagnostic interviews and assessments for Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder (PTSD).
